
---
## Section: seg_5

#### Statistics (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The science that deals with the collection, analysis, interpretation, and presentation of data is called ___
**Answer**: Statistics

#### Statistics (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best states the goal of statistics in this course?
A. Understanding your data so you can make better decisions
B. Memorizing many formulas and doing lots of calculations by hand
C. Always examining an entire population instead of a sample
D. Avoiding the use of calculators or computers when working with data
**Answer**: A

#### Descriptive Statistics (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Organizing and summarizing data by graphing or using numbers is called ___
**Answer**: Descriptive Statistics

#### Descriptive Statistics (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an example of descriptive statistics?
A. Computing the average of exam scores for a class
B. Using probability to decide how confident a conclusion is
C. Predicting rain using weather probabilities
D. Selecting 500 doctors at random for a study
**Answer**: A

#### Inferential Statistics (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Formal methods for drawing conclusions from data are called ___
**Answer**: Inferential Statistics

#### Inferential Statistics (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: After studying probability, using formal methods to draw conclusions from a sample to a population is called what?
A. Inferential statistics
B. Descriptive statistics
C. Sampling
D. Numerical variable
**Answer**: A

#### Statistical Inference (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Using probability to decide how confident we can be that our conclusions are correct is called ___
**Answer**: Statistical Inference

#### Statistical Inference (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When we judge how confident a conclusion is by using probability, we are doing what?
A. Statistical inference
B. Making a dot plot
C. Defining a variable
D. Choosing a representative sample
**Answer**: A

#### Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The mathematical tool used to study randomness and the chance of an event is ___
**Answer**: Probability

#### Probability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a fair coin, what is the expected theoretical probability of heads in one toss?
A. 0.25
B. 0.5
C. 0.75
D. 1.0
**Answer**: B

#### Population (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is the entire collection of persons, things, or objects under study
**Answer**: Population

#### Population (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a study of average money spent by first year students at ABC College, which is the population?
A. All first year students at ABC College this term
B. The 100 students surveyed
C. The average amount spent by the surveyed students
D. The amount spent by one student
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Selecting a portion of a larger population to study in order to gain information is called ___
**Answer**: Sampling

#### Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation best describes sampling?
A. Taking an opinion poll of 1,000–2,000 people to represent the country
B. Computing the average for every student in the school
C. Defining weight in pounds for each person
D. Calculating a population parameter
**Answer**: A

#### Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The selected subset of the population used to study the population is a ___
**Answer**: Sample

#### Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a study where 500 doctors are selected at random from a directory, the 500 doctors are the:
A. Sample
B. Population
C. Parameter
D. Statistic
**Answer**: A

#### Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A number that represents a property of the sample is called a ___
**Answer**: Statistic

#### Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which of the following is a statistic?
A. The average cumulative GPA of students in the study who graduated last year
B. The average cumulative GPA of all students who graduated last year
C. All students who graduated last year
D. The cumulative GPA of one student
**Answer**: A

#### Parameter (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A numerical characteristic of the entire population that can be estimated by a statistic is a ___
**Answer**: Parameter

#### Parameter (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a parameter in the ABC College spending study?
A. The average amount spent by all first year students at ABC College this term
B. The average amount spent by the 100 students surveyed
C. The 100 randomly selected first year students
D. The amounts $150, $200, and $225
**Answer**: A

#### Representative Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ contains the characteristics of the population
**Answer**: Representative Sample

#### Representative Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The accuracy of a statistic in estimating a parameter depends mainly on what?
A. How well the sample represents the population
B. How many formulas are memorized
C. Whether the variable is categorical
D. Whether a calculator is used
**Answer**: A

#### Variable (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a characteristic or measurement that can be determined for each member of a population
**Answer**: Variable

#### Variable (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the ABC College study, which is a variable?
A. The amount of money spent (excluding books) by one first year student
B. The list $150, $200, and $225
C. All first year students at ABC College this term
D. The average spent by all first year students
**Answer**: A

#### Numerical Variable (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A variable that takes on values with equal units, such as weight in pounds or time in hours, is a ___
**Answer**: Numerical Variable

#### Numerical Variable (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If X is the number of points earned by one math student, X is a:
A. Numerical variable
B. Categorical variable
C. Parameter
D. Statistic
**Answer**: A

#### Categorical Variable (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A variable that places a person or thing into a category is a ___
**Answer**: Categorical Variable

#### Categorical Variable (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If Y is a person's party affiliation with values like Republican, Democrat, or Independent, Y is a:
A. Categorical variable
B. Numerical variable
C. Parameter
D. Statistic
**Answer**: A

#### Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The actual values of a variable resulting from sampling are called ___
**Answer**: Data

#### Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which of the following are data in the ABC College study?
A. The amounts $150, $200, and $225
B. The amount spent by one student (as a concept)
C. The average amount spent by the surveyed students
D. All first year students at ABC College
**Answer**: A

#### Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The arithmetic average of a set of numbers, found by adding the values and dividing by the number of values, is the ___
**Answer**: Mean

#### Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If your exam scores are 86, 75, and 92, what is your mean score to one decimal place?
A. 84.3
B. 86.0
C. 75.0
D. 92.0
**Answer**: A

#### Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The fraction of the total in a given category is called a ___
**Answer**: Proportion

#### Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If 22 of 40 students are men, what is the proportion of men?
A. 22/40
B. 18/40
C. 22
D. 40/22
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_7

#### Qualitative Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Hair color or blood type are examples of ___.
**Answer**: qualitative data

#### Qualitative Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is qualitative data?
A. Hair color
B. Pulse rate
C. Number of phone calls per day
D. Backpack weight
**Answer**: A

#### Categorical Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: ___ is another term for qualitative data.
**Answer**: categorical data

#### Categorical Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is categorical (qualitative) data?
A. Blood type
B. Distance from home to a store
C. Number of students in a class
D. Weight of a backpack
**Answer**: A

#### Quantitative Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: ___ data are always numbers from counting or measuring.
**Answer**: quantitative data

#### Quantitative Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is quantitative data?
A. Amount of money
B. Hair color
C. Car type
D. Street name
**Answer**: A

#### Quantitative Discrete Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: All data that are the result of counting are called ___.
**Answer**: quantitative discrete data

#### Quantitative Discrete Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is quantitative discrete data?
A. Number of cars in a parking lot
B. Lengths of phone calls
C. Backpack weights
D. Distances to a grocery store
**Answer**: A

#### Quantitative Continuous Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Measured weights or lengths that may include decimals are ___.
**Answer**: quantitative continuous data

#### Quantitative Continuous Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is quantitative continuous data?
A. Time (in minutes) of a phone call
B. Number of calls per day
C. Number of books carried
D. Number of students in a town
**Answer**: A

#### Pie Chart (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ displays category wedges proportional to percentages and requires percentages that add to 100% with no missing categories.
**Answer**: pie chart

#### Pie Chart (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which graph cannot be used when percentages exceed 100% because people can be in more than one category?
A. Pie chart
B. Bar graph
C. Pareto chart
D. Histogram
**Answer**: A

#### Bar Graph (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a ___, bar length is proportional to the number or percent in each category.
**Answer**: bar graph

#### Bar Graph (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which display has bars that may be vertical or horizontal and need not be sorted by size?
A. Bar graph
B. Pie chart
C. Pareto chart
D. Histogram
**Answer**: A

#### Pareto Chart (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a bar graph with categories sorted from largest to smallest.
**Answer**: Pareto chart

#### Pareto Chart (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which graph makes it easier to compare categories by ordering them from largest to smallest?
A. Pareto chart
B. Pie chart
C. Bar graph with alphabetical order
D. Histogram
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Percentages or proportions shown alongside counts are called ___.
**Answer**: relative frequencies

#### Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why are relative frequencies helpful when comparing two colleges with different total enrollments?
A. They let you compare categories despite different totals
B. They guarantee random samples
C. They make graphs 3D
D. They ensure percentages exceed 100%
**Answer**: A

#### Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a subset used to represent a population and should have the same characteristics.
**Answer**: sample

#### Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is true about a good sample?
A. It should be representative of the population
B. It must include everyone in the population
C. It must be chosen by convenience
D. It must have exactly 1,500 people
**Answer**: A

#### Random Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In ___, each member of the population initially has an equal chance of being selected.
**Answer**: random sampling

#### Random Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which term names the idea that each population member starts with an equal chance of selection?
A. Random sampling
B. Convenience sampling
C. Non-response
D. Confounding
**Answer**: A

#### Simple Random Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Any group of n individuals is equally likely to be chosen in a ___.
**Answer**: simple random sample

#### Simple Random Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Putting all names in a hat and picking three is what type of sampling?
A. Simple random sample
B. Systematic sample
C. Stratified sample
D. Convenience sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Stratified Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ divides the population into strata and takes a proportionate number from each.
**Answer**: stratified sample

#### Stratified Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Selecting 25 freshmen, 25 sophomores, 25 juniors, and 25 seniors is an example of which sampling method?
A. Stratified sample
B. Cluster sample
C. Systematic sample
D. Convenience sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Strata (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In stratified sampling, the groups are called ___.
**Answer**: strata

#### Strata (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Departments used to group a college before sampling are examples of
A. Strata
B. Clusters
C. Samples
D. Variables
**Answer**: A

#### Cluster Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a ___, entire groups (clusters) are randomly selected and all members in selected groups are included.
**Answer**: cluster sample

#### Cluster Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Randomly choosing four departments and surveying everyone in those departments is which method?
A. Cluster sample
B. Stratified sample
C. Systematic sample
D. Simple random sample
**Answer**: A

#### Systematic Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Randomly choose a start and then take every nth member; this is a ___.
**Answer**: systematic sample

#### Systematic Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Choosing every 50th student after a random start is which sampling method?
A. Systematic sample
B. Cluster sample
C. Stratified sample
D. Convenience sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Convenience Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Interviewing people who happen to be in a store is ___ and may be biased.
**Answer**: convenience sampling

#### Convenience Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A computer store interviews shoppers who happen to be browsing. What sampling is this?
A. Convenience sampling
B. Simple random sample
C. Stratified sample
D. Systematic sample
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In ___, a selected member goes back into the population and may be chosen again.
**Answer**: sampling with replacement

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: True random sampling is done with which approach?
A. Sampling with replacement
B. Sampling without replacement
C. Cluster sampling
D. Convenience sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In ___, a member of the population may be chosen only once.
**Answer**: sampling without replacement

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When the population is large and the sample is small, sampling without replacement is approximately the same as which method?
A. Sampling with replacement
B. Convenience sampling
C. Cluster sampling
D. Self-selected sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Error caused by the sampling process itself is called ___.
**Answer**: sampling error

#### Sampling Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a sampling error?
A. Using too small a sample
B. A broken scale mismeasures weights
C. Two variables cannot be separated
D. People refuse to answer
**Answer**: A

#### Nonsampling Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An error from a factor not related to sampling, such as a defective counting device, is a ___.
**Answer**: nonsampling error

#### Nonsampling Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a nonsampling error?
A. A faulty counter miscounts attendees
B. Using convenience sampling
C. Picking every 10th name
D. Small sample size
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Bias (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When some members are less likely to be chosen than others, ___ occurs.
**Answer**: sampling bias

#### Sampling Bias (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If a sample method gives some population members no real chance to be selected, this is
A. Sampling bias
B. Confounding
C. Causality
D. Random sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Problems With Samples (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A sample that is not representative of the population is ___ and gives inaccurate, invalid results.
**Answer**: biased

#### Problems With Samples (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Estimating average GPA using only honor students is a problem because the sample is
A. Biased
B. Random
C. A census
D. Large enough
**Answer**: A

#### Self-Selected Samples (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Responses only from those who choose to respond (for example, call-in surveys) are ___.
**Answer**: self-selected samples

#### Self-Selected Samples (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a self-selected sample?
A. An online poll that anyone can choose to answer
B. A simple random sample from a roster
C. Choosing every 25th person from a list
D. Randomly selecting departments and surveying all members
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Size Issues (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Samples that are too small may be ___; larger samples are better when possible.
**Answer**: unreliable

#### Sample Size Issues (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about sample size is true?
A. Larger samples are better if possible
B. A big sample removes all bias
C. Sample size never matters
D. Small samples always suffice
**Answer**: A

#### Undue Influence (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Asking questions in a way that influences the response is called ___.
**Answer**: undue influence

#### Undue Influence (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an example of undue influence?
A. A leading question that pushes a preferred answer
B. Randomly picking a starting point
C. Taking every 50th name
D. Recording both counts and percentages
**Answer**: A

#### Non-Response (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Refusal or failure to participate so that responses may not represent the population is ___.
**Answer**: non-response

#### Non-Response (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Many selected subjects do not answer a survey. This problem is called
A. Non-response
B. Causality
C. Confounding
D. Convenience
**Answer**: A

#### Causality (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A relationship between two variables does not imply ___.
**Answer**: causality

#### Causality (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is correct?
A. Correlation does not imply causation
B. Any relationship implies causation
C. Larger samples cause causation
D. Random sampling proves causation
**Answer**: A

#### Self-Funded Or Self-Interest Studies (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A study performed by an interested party to support its claim is a ___ and must be evaluated for impartiality.
**Answer**: self-funded or self-interest study

#### Self-Funded Or Self-Interest Studies (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How should you treat a self-funded study?
A. Evaluate it on its merits; do not automatically accept or reject
B. Assume it is always good
C. Assume it is always bad
D. Ignore it entirely
**Answer**: A

#### Misleading Use Of Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Improperly displayed graphs, incomplete data, or lack of context are examples of ___.
**Answer**: misleading use of data

#### Misleading Use Of Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which practice could mislead readers?
A. Omitting a large 'Other/Unknown' category from a pie chart so percents do not sum to 100%
B. Including both counts and percentages
C. Sorting bars from largest to smallest
D. Using random sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Confounding (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When the effects of multiple factors on a response cannot be separated, ___ makes conclusions difficult.
**Answer**: confounding

#### Confounding (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Two factors both affect an outcome, and their separate effects cannot be determined. This is
A. Confounding
B. Sampling bias
C. Non-response
D. Relative frequency
**Answer**: A

#### Variation In Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Natural differences in repeated measurements, such as 16-ounce cans not all having exactly 16 ounces, are called ___.
**Answer**: variation in data

#### Variation In Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an example of variation in data?
A. Eight 16-ounce cans measured at 15.8, 16.1, 15.2, etc.
B. Picking every 50th name
C. Comparing two departments
D. Listing percentages by category
**Answer**: A

#### Variation In Samples (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Two random samples from the same population will likely differ; this is ___.
**Answer**: variation in samples

#### Variation In Samples (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is true about random samples from the same population?
A. They will likely be different from each other
B. They must be identical
C. They are always biased
D. They prove causation
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Size (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The number of observations in a sample is called the ___.
**Answer**: sample size

#### Sample Size (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In polling, a sample of roughly what size is often considered large enough if random and well done?
A. 1,200 to 1,500
B. 12 to 15
C. 120 to 150
D. 12,000 to 15,000
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_9

#### Level Of Measurement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The way a set of data is measured is called its ___.
**Answer**: level of measurement

#### Level Of Measurement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why must researchers be familiar with levels of measurement?
A. Not every statistical operation can be used with every set of data.
B. It guarantees larger sample sizes.
C. It eliminates the need to round answers.
D. It turns qualitative data into quantitative data.
**Answer**: A

#### Nominal Scale Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Categories, colors, names, labels, and yes/no responses are examples of data measured on the ___.
**Answer**: nominal scale level

#### Nominal Scale Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is nominal level data?
A. Smartphone company names
B. A ranking of the top five national parks
C. Celsius temperatures
D. Exam scores out of 100 with a meaningful zero
**Answer**: A

#### Ordinal Scale Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Survey responses of "excellent," "good," "satisfactory," and "unsatisfactory" are measured on the ___.
**Answer**: ordinal scale level

#### Ordinal Scale Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes ordinal level data?
A. The data can be ordered, but differences between values cannot be measured.
B. The data are not ordered and cannot be used in calculations.
C. Ratios like "twice as much" are meaningful.
D. There is no ordering, but differences are meaningful.
**Answer**: A

#### Interval Scale Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Celsius and Fahrenheit temperatures are examples of the ___ because differences make sense but there is no true zero.
**Answer**: interval scale level

#### Interval Scale Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which comparison is not meaningful for interval level data?
A. The difference between 80°F and 70°F is 10°F.
B. 80°F is four times as hot as 20°F.
C. Temperatures can be ordered from coldest to warmest.
D. Some temperatures can be below zero.
**Answer**: B

#### Ratio Scale Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Machine-graded exam scores out of 100 that allow statements like "80 is four times 20" are measured on the ___.
**Answer**: ratio scale level

#### Ratio Scale Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is valid for ratio level data?
A. Ratios such as 80 is four times 20 are meaningful.
B. Zero has no meaning as a starting point.
C. Differences between values cannot be measured.
D. The data cannot be ordered.
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is the number of times a value of the data occurs.
**Answer**: frequency

#### Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a frequency table, the sum of the frequency column equals the:
A. total number of observations
B. number of distinct data values
C. mean of the data set
D. frequency of the most common value
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is the ratio of a value’s frequency to the total number of outcomes.
**Answer**: relative frequency

#### Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The sum of all relative frequencies in a table should be:
A. approximately 1
B. exactly 100
C. equal to the number of rows
D. approximately 0.5
**Answer**: A

#### Cumulative Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: ___ is found by adding all previous relative frequencies to the current row.
**Answer**: cumulative relative frequency

#### Cumulative Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The last entry in a cumulative relative frequency column should be:
A. 0
B. 0.5
C. approximately 1
D. the sum of the frequencies
**Answer**: C

#### Frequency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ lists distinct data values (often in ascending order) and their frequencies.
**Answer**: frequency table

#### Frequency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which name best fits a table that lists data values and their frequencies, and may include relative and cumulative relative frequencies?
A. Frequency table
B. List of raw data
C. Interval
D. Level of measurement
**Answer**: A

#### Rounding Off Rules (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Round only the ___, carrying it one more decimal place than the original data.
**Answer**: final answer

#### Rounding Off Rules (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If you must round intermediate results, you should carry them to:
A. at least twice as many decimal places as in the final answer
B. exactly the same decimal places as the final answer
C. one more decimal place than the original data
D. no decimal places
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_11

#### Randomized Experiment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A study in which the researcher manipulates the explanatory variable and measures changes in the response variable is a ___.
**Answer**: randomized experiment

#### Randomized Experiment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which scenario is a randomized experiment?
A. Men are randomly assigned to take aspirin or a placebo, and heart attacks are counted.
B. People who already take vitamin E are compared to those who do not.
C. Birth order and personality are recorded without assigning anything.
D. Existing records are summarized with no treatments given.
**Answer**: A

#### Explanatory Variable (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The variable that causes change in another variable is the ___.
**Answer**: explanatory variable

#### Explanatory Variable (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the aspirin study, what is the explanatory variable?
A. Oral medication
B. Heart attack occurrence
C. Men aged 50–84
D. Number of researchers
**Answer**: A

#### Response Variable (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The variable affected by changes in the explanatory variable is the ___.
**Answer**: response variable

#### Response Variable (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the scented mask maze study, what is the response variable?
A. Type of scent
B. Time to complete the maze
C. Age of the subjects
D. Order of mask wearing
**Answer**: B

#### Treatments (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The different values of the explanatory variable are called ___.
**Answer**: treatments

#### Treatments (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What are the treatments in the scented mask study?
A. Floral-scented mask and unscented mask
B. Fast and slow completion times
C. Positive vs. negative scent impressions
D. Three trials vs. six trials
**Answer**: A

#### Experimental Unit (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A single object or individual to be measured is an ___.
**Answer**: experimental unit

#### Experimental Unit (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the aspirin study, what are the experimental units?
A. The individual men
B. The pills
C. The heart attacks
D. The journals
**Answer**: A

#### Lurking Variables (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Additional variables that can cloud a study are called ___.
**Answer**: lurking variables

#### Lurking Variables (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the vitamin E example, which is a lurking variable that could affect health?
A. Exercise habits
B. Vitamin E use
C. Overall health outcome
D. Random assignment method
**Answer**: A

#### Random Assignment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assigning experimental units to treatment groups randomly to spread lurking variables equally is called ___.
**Answer**: random assignment

#### Random Assignment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the main purpose of random assignment in an experiment?
A. To ensure the sample represents the population
B. To balance lurking variables across treatment groups
C. To increase the sample size
D. To guarantee blinding
**Answer**: B

#### Control Group (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A treatment group set aside to receive a placebo is called the ___.
**Answer**: control group

#### Control Group (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why include a control group in a randomized experiment?
A. To guarantee double blinding
B. To balance the effects of being in the study apart from active treatments
C. To increase the number of treatments
D. To eliminate the need for random assignment
**Answer**: B

#### Placebo (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A treatment that cannot influence the response variable is a ___.
**Answer**: placebo

#### Placebo (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the aspirin heart attack study, what did the control group receive?
A. A higher dose of aspirin
B. A placebo
C. An exercise program
D. Vitamin E
**Answer**: B

#### Blinding (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When a person in a study does not know who receives the active treatment or the placebo, this is ___.
**Answer**: blinding

#### Blinding (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In which study is subject blinding not possible?
A. A pill study with identical-looking tablets
B. A floral-scent mask study where subjects can smell the scent
C. A double-blind drug trial
D. A study with a placebo control
**Answer**: B

#### Double-Blind Experiment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An experiment where both subjects and the researchers working with them are blinded is a ___.
**Answer**: double-blind experiment

#### Double-Blind Experiment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes a double-blind experiment?
A. Neither subjects nor interacting researchers know who receives active treatment
B. Only subjects are unaware of their treatment
C. Only data analysts are unaware of treatment labels
D. Blinding is unnecessary if random assignment is used
**Answer**: A

#### Institutional Review Board (IRB) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: All planned studies with human participants must be approved in advance by the ___.
**Answer**: Institutional Review Board (IRB)

#### Institutional Review Board (IRB) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is a key role of an IRB?
A. Approving planned studies to protect human subjects
B. Analyzing data for statistical errors
C. Providing funding for research
D. Recruiting study participants
**Answer**: A

#### Risk Minimization (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The requirement that risks to participants be minimized and reasonable relative to benefits is called ___.
**Answer**: risk minimization

#### Risk Minimization (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does risk minimization require in human-subjects research?
A. Risks must be minimized and reasonable compared to benefits
B. Researchers must promise zero risk
C. As much data as possible must be collected regardless of risk
D. IRB approval can be skipped if risks are small
**Answer**: A

#### Informed Consent (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Clearly explaining risks to participants, obtaining written consent, and keeping documentation is called ___.
**Answer**: informed consent

#### Informed Consent (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which practice best demonstrates informed consent?
A. Subjects sign a form stating risks were explained
B. Assuming consent if no one objects
C. Hiding risks to avoid biasing responses
D. Using leftover biological samples without notice
**Answer**: A

#### Privacy Protection (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Guarding data collected from individuals to protect their identities is called ___.
**Answer**: privacy protection

#### Privacy Protection (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which action aligns with privacy protection?
A. Securing data to prevent revealing participants’ identities
B. Publishing raw data with enough details to re-identify people
C. Sharing participants’ addresses with sponsors
D. Skipping data security to speed analysis
**Answer**: A

#### Falsifying Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Creating or altering data, changing instruments without reporting, or misrepresenting subject numbers is called ___.
**Answer**: falsifying data

#### Falsifying Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which action is an example of falsifying data described in the section?
A. Altering data in an existing dataset to fit expectations
B. Getting advance approval from an IRB
C. Using random assignment to form groups
D. Including a control group with a placebo
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_13

#### Systematic Sampling Technique (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Randomly pick one person on the class list, then move down four names each time to mark 12 names. This is an example of ___.
**Answer**: systematic sampling technique

#### Systematic Sampling Technique (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the movie survey, selecting a random starting student and then every fourth name until 12 are selected is best described as which method?
A. Systematic sampling technique
B. Simple random sampling
C. Convenience sampling
D. Stratified sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A table listing the number of movies with columns for frequency, relative frequency, and cumulative relative frequency is a ___.
**Answer**: relative frequency table

#### Relative Frequency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the tables you complete for number of movies?
A. A relative frequency table summarizing categories with counts, percents, and cumulative percents
B. A scatter plot of movies and students
C. A list of raw data only
D. A bar chart without percents
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the tables, the count of data values in a category is the ___.
**Answer**: frequency

#### Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the class tables, 'frequency' refers to which quantity?
A. The number of data values in a category
B. The percent of the dataset in a category
C. The running total of percents up to a category
D. The average number of movies
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the tables, the percent of the dataset in a category is the ___.
**Answer**: relative frequency

#### Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Relative frequency gives you which measure for a category?
A. Percent of the dataset in that category
B. Total number of students surveyed
C. Running total of percents through that category
D. Difference between the largest and smallest values
**Answer**: A

#### Cumulative Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The running total of relative frequencies up to and including a category is the ___.
**Answer**: cumulative relative frequency

#### Cumulative Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: To find the percent of data that is at most two movies, which column should you use?
A. Frequency
B. Relative Frequency
C. Cumulative Relative Frequency
D. Raw Data
**Answer**: C

#### Data Grouping (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Using single values (0, 1, 2, …) versus combined intervals (0–1, 2–3, 4–5, 6–7+) to organize the data is called ___.
**Answer**: data grouping

#### Data Grouping (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How do the two tables group the number of movies differently?
A. One uses single-value categories; the other combines values into intervals
B. One shows only averages; the other shows only totals
C. One excludes the 7+ category; the other excludes 0
D. One is more correct than the other
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_15

#### Simple Random Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In ___, each member of the population is given a number and a random number generator selects the labels for the sample.
**Answer**: simple random sampling

#### Simple Random Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which step is required to select a simple random sample of 15 restaurants from the provided list?
A. Assign numbers to all restaurants and use a random number generator to pick 15.
B. Choose 25% from each city and combine them.
C. Randomly pick two cities and include all restaurants in those cities.
D. Pick a random start and then choose every kth restaurant.
**Answer**: A

#### Systematic Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In systematic sampling, after choosing a random start and computing k, you select every ___ individual on the list.
**Answer**: kth

#### Systematic Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: You list all restaurants, randomly start at the 7th, then select every 20th until you have 15. What method is this?
A. Simple random sampling
B. Stratified sampling
C. Cluster sampling
D. Systematic sampling
**Answer**: D

#### Stratified Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To take a stratified sample by city, divide the restaurants into ___ and randomly select a proportion from each.
**Answer**: strata

#### Stratified Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The lab asks for 25% of the restaurants from each city. Which sampling method is this?
A. Simple random sampling
B. Stratified sampling
C. Cluster sampling
D. Systematic sampling
**Answer**: B

#### Cluster Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In cluster sampling, you randomly select clusters and then include ___ in the chosen clusters.
**Answer**: every individual

#### Cluster Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: You randomly select two cities from the list and include all restaurants in those cities. What method is this?
A. Cluster sampling
B. Stratified sampling
C. Systematic sampling
D. Simple random sampling
**Answer**: A

#### Random Sampling (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A sample where each restaurant has an equal chance of being chosen uses ___ sampling.
**Answer**: random sampling

#### Random Sampling (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which procedure gives every restaurant on the list the same chance to be selected?
A. Use a random number generator to select labels from the full list.
B. Choose only restaurants with entree cost under $10.
C. Select two cities and include all restaurants in them.
D. Ask for volunteers from the list.
**Answer**: A

#### Population (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the restaurant lab, the ___ is all the restaurants listed for sampling.
**Answer**: population

#### Population (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the population for the restaurant sampling activities?
A. All restaurants listed in the provided tables
B. The 15 restaurants you selected
C. All restaurants in the world
D. The random number generator outputs
**Answer**: A

#### Sample (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The 15 restaurants you record in the simple random sample table form a ___ of the listed restaurants.
**Answer**: sample

#### Sample (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement defines a sample in this context?
A. The entire set of restaurants being studied
B. A subset of restaurants chosen from the population
C. The value of k in a systematic sample
D. All clusters formed by entree cost
**Answer**: B

## Section: seg_19

#### Stem-And-Leaf Graph (Stemplot) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a simple graph for small data sets that divides each value into a stem and a leaf to give an exact picture of the data.
**Answer**: stem-and-leaf graph (stemplot)

#### Stem-And-Leaf Graph (Stemplot) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which graph displays a single small data set by dividing each value into a stem and a leaf?
A. Line graph
B. Bar graph
C. Stem-and-leaf graph (stemplot)
D. Side-by-side stem-and-leaf plot
**Answer**: C

#### Stem (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a stemplot, the ___ is the part of the number before the final significant digit and is listed vertically from smallest to largest.
**Answer**: stem

#### Stem (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a stemplot, what is the stem for the number 432?
A. 4
B. 43
C. 432
D. 32
**Answer**: B

#### Leaf (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a stemplot, the ___ is the final significant digit written next to its corresponding stem in increasing order.
**Answer**: leaf

#### Leaf (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: According to the examples, what is the leaf for 5,432 in a stemplot?
A. 2
B. 5
C. 32
D. 543
**Answer**: A

#### Side-By-Side Stem-And-Leaf Plot (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ compares two data sets by placing leaves on both the left and right of shared stems.
**Answer**: side-by-side stem-and-leaf plot

#### Side-By-Side Stem-And-Leaf Plot (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What distinguishes a side-by-side stemplot from a regular stemplot?
A. Two sets of leaves share the same stems, with leaves on both sides.
B. Frequencies are connected by line segments on a graph.
C. Bars are separated from each other, either vertical or horizontal.
D. It shows proportions by age group on the y-axis.
**Answer**: A

#### Outlier (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An ___ is an observation that does not fit the rest of the data and appears not to follow the graph’s pattern.
**Answer**: outlier

#### Outlier (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the supermarket distance data, which value may be an outlier?
A. 3.3
B. 4.5
C. 6.5
D. 12.3
**Answer**: D

#### Line Graph (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the line graph described in this section, the x-axis shows ___ and the y-axis shows frequency points.
**Answer**: data values

#### Line Graph (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes the line graph used in this section?
A. The x-axis consists of data values, the y-axis of frequency points, and points are connected by line segments.
B. The x-axis shows age groups, and separated bars show proportions.
C. Stems are listed vertically and leaves are written next to them in increasing order.
D. Two data sets share the same stems with leaves on both sides.
**Answer**: A

#### Bar Graph (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ consists of separated bars that may be vertical or horizontal.
**Answer**: bar graph

#### Bar Graph (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a characteristic of bar graphs in this section?
A. Bars are separated and can be vertical or horizontal.
B. The x-axis is data values and points are connected by line segments.
C. Leaves are the final significant digits listed next to stems.
D. Two sets of leaves share the same stems.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_21

#### Histogram (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A histogram consists of ___ boxes.
**Answer**: contiguous (adjoining)

#### Histogram (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: As a rule of thumb, a histogram is most appropriate when a data set has about how many values or more?
A. 30
B. 50
C. 100
D. 1000
**Answer**: C

#### Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Frequency is the number of times an ___ occurs.
**Answer**: answer

#### Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes frequency?
A. The number of times a value occurs
B. The proportion of a value in the sample
C. The midpoint of a class
D. The total number of data values
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Relative frequency RF equals f divided by ___.
**Answer**: n

#### Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Mr. Ahab’s class, 3 of 40 students scored 90–100%. What is the relative frequency?
A. 0.075
B. 0.75
C. 0.0075
D. 0.30
**Answer**: A

#### Class Interval (Class) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a histogram, the bars or intervals are called ___.
**Answer**: classes

#### Class Interval (Class) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What are the grouped ranges along the horizontal axis of a histogram called?
A. Classes
B. Categories
C. Coordinates
D. Medians
**Answer**: A

#### Class Width (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Class width = (ending value − starting point) ÷ number of ___.
**Answer**: bars

#### Class Width (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the starting point is 0.5, the ending value is 6.5, and you choose 6 bars, what is the class width?
A. 1
B. 6
C. 1.5
D. 0.5
**Answer**: A

#### Starting Point (Histogram) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Choose a starting point less than the smallest data value and carried to one more ___ place than the value with the most decimal places.
**Answer**: decimal

#### Starting Point (Histogram) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If all data are integers and the smallest value is 2, which starting point is convenient?
A. 2
B. 1.5
C. 0.5
D. 2.5
**Answer**: B

#### Class Boundaries Convention (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Carrying boundaries to one additional ___ place helps ensure no data value falls on a boundary.
**Answer**: decimal

#### Class Boundaries Convention (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Under the boundary convention, a value that falls exactly on a class boundary is counted:
A. In the class on the left boundary only
B. In both neighboring classes
C. In neither class
D. In the class on the right boundary only
**Answer**: A

#### Number Of Classes (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A guideline is to take the square root of the number of data values and then ___ to a whole number.
**Answer**: round

#### Number Of Classes (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For clarity, many histograms use how many bars (classes)?
A. 2 to 4
B. 5 to 15
C. 20 to 30
D. At least 50
**Answer**: B

#### Continuous Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Continuous data are obtained by ___.
**Answer**: measurement

#### Continuous Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Heights in inches (measured) are what type of data?
A. Continuous
B. Discrete
C. Categorical
D. Binary
**Answer**: A

#### Discrete Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Discrete data are obtained by ___.
**Answer**: counting

#### Discrete Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The number of books bought by 50 students is what type of data?
A. Discrete
B. Continuous
C. Qualitative
D. Time series
**Answer**: A

#### Discrete Data Class Boundaries (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For integer data, subtract ___ from the smallest value and add the same amount to the largest value.
**Answer**: 0.5

#### Discrete Data Class Boundaries (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For integer data from 1 to 6, which starting and ending values are convenient for the histogram?
A. 0.5 and 6.5
B. 1 and 6
C. 0 and 7
D. 0.05 and 6.05
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency Polygon (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: After plotting the data points for a frequency polygon, draw ___ to connect them.
**Answer**: line segments

#### Frequency Polygon (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A frequency polygon is analogous to which type of graph?
A. Line graph
B. Bar chart
C. Pie chart
D. Scatter plot
**Answer**: A

#### Time Series Graph (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a time series graph, the horizontal axis plots ___.
**Answer**: time

#### Time Series Graph (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement describes a time series graph?
A. Time on the horizontal axis and values on the vertical axis; points connected in order
B. Values on the horizontal axis and time on the vertical axis; bars not connected
C. Random ordering of data points; no connections
D. Only averages are plotted; no time component
**Answer**: A

#### Uses Of A Time Series Graph (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Time series graphs make ___ easy to spot.
**Answer**: trends

#### Uses Of A Time Series Graph (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why are time series graphs important when data are recorded over time?
A. They make trends easy to spot
B. They hide changes to reduce noise
C. They show only the median value
D. They require data to be random
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_23

#### Measures Of Location (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Common measures of location are ___ and percentiles.
**Answer**: quartiles

#### Measures Of Location (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which pair are common measures of the location of the data?
A. Quartiles and percentiles
B. Variance and range
C. Mean and standard deviation
D. Mode and IQR
**Answer**: A

#### Percentiles (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Percentiles divide ordered data into ___.
**Answer**: hundredths

#### Percentiles (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Scoring in the 90th percentile on an exam means:
A. You scored 90% correct
B. About 90% of scores are the same or less than yours
C. Your score is exactly at the median
D. Only 10% scored below you
**Answer**: B

#### Quartiles (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Quartiles divide ordered data into ___.
**Answer**: quarters

#### Quartiles (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is correct about quartiles?
A. Q1 is the 75th percentile
B. Q3 is the 75th percentile
C. Quartiles divide data into tenths
D. The median is Q3
**Answer**: B

#### First Quartile (Q1) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The first quartile (Q1) is the median of the ___ half of the ordered data.
**Answer**: lower

#### First Quartile (Q1) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Another name for Q1 is the:
A. 50th percentile
B. 25th percentile
C. 75th percentile
D. Minimum
**Answer**: B

#### Median (Second Quartile, 50th Percentile) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The median is the ___ percentile.
**Answer**: 50th

#### Median (Second Quartile, 50th Percentile) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about the median?
A. It must be one of the observed data values
B. It splits ordered data into two halves
C. It equals Q3 for any symmetric data set
D. It is always the average of the two middle values
**Answer**: B

#### Third Quartile (Q3) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The third quartile (Q3) is the ___ percentile.
**Answer**: 75th

#### Third Quartile (Q3) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Q3 is the median of the:
A. Lower half of the data
B. Upper half of the data
C. Entire data set
D. Middle 50% of the data
**Answer**: B

#### Interquartile Range (IQR) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The interquartile range is computed as ___.
**Answer**: Q3 - Q1

#### Interquartile Range (IQR) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does the IQR represent?
A. The spread of the middle 50% of the data
B. The difference between maximum and minimum
C. The average distance from the mean
D. The number of quartiles in the data
**Answer**: A

#### Potential Outlier (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A value more than 1.5 IQRs above Q3 or below Q1 is called a ___.
**Answer**: potential outlier

#### Potential Outlier (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which rule is used to flag potential outliers?
A. Values beyond mean ± 1.5(SD)
B. Values below Q1 − 1.5(IQR) or above Q3 + 1.5(IQR)
C. Values beyond median ± IQR
D. Values below Q1 or above Q3
**Answer**: B

#### Five-Number Summary (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The five-number summary lists Minimum, Q1, ___, Q3, and Maximum.
**Answer**: Median

#### Five-Number Summary (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is the correct order of a five-number summary?
A. Median, Q1, Q3, Minimum, Maximum
B. Minimum, Q1, Median, Q3, Maximum
C. Minimum, Mean, Median, Mode, Maximum
D. Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, Maximum
**Answer**: B

#### Kth Percentile Index Formula (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a data set of size n, the index for the kth percentile is i = ___.
**Answer**: (k/100)(n + 1)

#### Kth Percentile Index Formula (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using i = (k/100)(n + 1), what is i for the 70th percentile when n = 29?
A. 20.0
B. 21.0
C. 21.7
D. 24.9
**Answer**: B

#### Percentile Rank Formula (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Percentile rank = ___ × 100 (with x below, y equal, and n total).
**Answer**: (x + 0.5y)/n

#### Percentile Rank Formula (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a list of 29 values, if 18 are below and 1 is equal to your value, the percentile rank is approximately:
A. 60th
B. 62nd
C. 64th
D. 68th
**Answer**: C

#### Interpreting Percentiles (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Whether a percentile is considered "good" or "bad" depends on the ___.
**Answer**: context

#### Interpreting Percentiles (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about interpreting percentiles is true?
A. Low percentiles always mean better outcomes
B. High percentiles always mean better outcomes
C. Whether a percentile is good or bad depends on the context
D. Percentiles cannot be used to compare values
**Answer**: C

#### Percentile Interpretation Elements (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A clear percentile interpretation should include the percent below and the percent ___ the percentile.
**Answer**: above

#### Percentile Interpretation Elements (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which item is NOT part of the recommended elements when interpreting a percentile?
A. The context of the situation
B. The sample mean
C. The data value at the percentile
D. The percent below the percentile
**Answer**: B

#### Box Plot (Box-And-Whisker Plot) (Q1)

**Answer**: five values

A. From Q1 to Q3
B. From the minimum to the maximum
C. From the median to the maximum
D. From Q1 to the median
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: first quartile

A. Minimum, first quartile, median, third quartile, maximum

C. Minimum, first quartile, median, mode, maximum
D. Mean, first quartile, median, third quartile, maximum
**Answer**: A

#### Minimum Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The smallest data value used as an axis endpoint in a box plot is the ___.

#### Minimum Value (Q2)

A. The smallest data value to which a whisker extends
B. The average of all data values
C. The left edge of the box
D. The value marking the middle of the box
**Answer**: A

#### Maximum Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The largest data value used as an axis endpoint in a box plot is the ___.

A. The maximum value
B. The mean
C. The third quartile
D. The interquartile range
**Answer**: A

#### First Quartile (Q1) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The left edge of the box in a box plot marks the ___.
**Answer**: first quartile (Q1)

#### First Quartile (Q1) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Approximately what percent of the data lie between the minimum and Q1?
A. 10%
B. 25%
C. 50%
D. 75%
**Answer**: B

#### Median (Q2) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a box plot, the median is often shown with a ___ line.

#### Median (Q2) (Q2)

A. It can lie between Q1 and Q3 or coincide with one of them.
B. It is always exactly at the center of the box.
C. It is outside the box at the highest value.
D. It equals the average of Q1 and Q3.

#### Third Quartile (Q3) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The right edge of the box in a box plot marks the ___.

#### Third Quartile (Q3) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Approximately what percent of the data lie between Q3 and the maximum?
A. 25%
B. 50%
C. 10%
D. 75%
**Answer**: A

#### Whiskers (Q1)

**Answer**: smallest and largest

#### Whiskers (Q2)

C. The box shrinks to a point at the median.
D. The median line is removed.
**Answer**: A

A. 18.0

D. 6.5

#### Middle 50% (Q2)

A. The rectangular box from Q1 to Q3
B. The whiskers only
C. The dashed median line
D. The entire axis from minimum to maximum
**Answer**: A

#### Tied Values In Box Plots (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If the median equals the third quartile, there will be no separate ___ inside the box.
**Answer**: median line

#### Tied Values In Box Plots (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the smallest value equals Q1 and the median equals Q3, how is the median displayed?
A. At the right edge of the box, coinciding with Q3
B. At the left whisker endpoint
C. As a dash in the exact center of the box
D. As a dot outside the box
**Answer**: A

#### Quartile Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Each quarter in a box plot represents approximately ___% of the data.
**Answer**: 25

#### Quartile Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What percentage of the data is between the first quartile and the largest value?
A. 25%
B. 50%
C. 75%
D. 100%
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_27

#### Arithmetic Mean (Average) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The arithmetic mean is found by adding all data values and dividing by the number of values; it is commonly called the ___.
**Answer**: average

#### Arithmetic Mean (Average) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which term means the sum of all data values divided by the number of values?
A. arithmetic mean (average)
B. median
C. mode
D. midpoint of an interval
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Mean (X̄) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample mean is denoted by ___ (pronounced "x bar").
**Answer**: x̄

#### Sample Mean (X̄) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which condition helps ensure that x̄ is a good estimate of μ?
A. The sample is truly random.
B. The sample is very small.
C. The data have many outliers.
D. The population is ordered smallest to largest.
**Answer**: A

#### Population Mean (Μ) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The population mean is represented by the Greek letter ___.
**Answer**: μ

#### Population Mean (Μ) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does μ represent?
A. The arithmetic mean of an entire population.
B. The median of a sample.
C. The mode of grouped data.
D. The midpoint of the middle interval.
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Calculation Using Frequencies (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When values repeat, you can find the mean by multiplying each distinct value by its ___ and dividing by the total number of data values.
**Answer**: frequency

#### Mean Calculation Using Frequencies (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which calculation finds the mean when values have frequencies?
A. Sum of (value × frequency) divided by total number of data values.
B. Sum of frequencies divided by number of distinct values.
C. Median of the distinct values.
D. Most frequent value only.
**Answer**: A

#### Median (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The median is generally a better measure of center when there are extreme values or ___.
**Answer**: outliers

#### Median (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For an even number of ordered data values, the median is the...
A. average of the two middle values.
B. most frequent value.
C. first value.
D. smallest value.
**Answer**: A

#### Median Location Formula (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The position of the median in ordered data is given by ___.
**Answer**: (n + 1) / 2

#### Median Location Formula (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If n = 40, what is the location of the median?
A. 20.5
B. 20
C. 21
D. 40
**Answer**: A

#### Mode (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A data set with two modes is called ___.
**Answer**: bimodal

#### Mode (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which measure can be used with qualitative data (for example, colors)?
A. mode
B. mean
C. median
D. midpoint
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a representation of grouped data that lists intervals and their corresponding frequencies.
**Answer**: frequency table

#### Frequency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes a frequency table?
A. It displays grouped intervals with their frequencies.
B. It lists every individual data value.
C. It shows only the median and mode.
D. It lists only relative frequencies without intervals.
**Answer**: A

#### Midpoint Of An Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The midpoint of an interval equals (lower boundary + upper boundary) divided by ___.
**Answer**: 2

#### Midpoint Of An Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the midpoint of the interval 68.5–74.5?
A. 71.5
B. 68.5
C. 74.5
D. 72.5
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Of A Grouped Frequency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The estimated mean from a grouped frequency table is computed as ___, where f is the frequency and m is the midpoint.
**Answer**: Σ(fm) / Σf

#### Mean Of A Grouped Frequency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why is the mean from a grouped frequency table only an estimate?
A. Because the individual data values are unknown; only intervals and frequencies are given.
B. Because the median is not available.
C. Because the mode is always larger than the mean.
D. Because the sample is not random.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_29

#### Symmetrical Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A distribution is called ___ if a vertical line can be drawn so the left and right sides are mirror images.
**Answer**: symmetrical

#### Symmetrical Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which shape describes a histogram whose left and right sides mirror each other across a vertical line?
A. Symmetrical
B. Skewed to the left
C. Skewed to the right
D. Bimodal
**Answer**: A

#### Skewed To The Left (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A distribution pulled out to the left is described as ___.
**Answer**: skewed to the left

#### Skewed To The Left (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the right-hand side of a histogram looks "chopped off" compared to the left, the distribution is:
A. Symmetrical
B. Skewed to the left
C. Skewed to the right
D. Unimodal
**Answer**: B

#### Skewed To The Right (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A distribution pulled out to the right is called ___.
**Answer**: skewed to the right

#### Skewed To The Right (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The data set 6; 7; 7; 7; 7; 8; 8; 8; 9; 10 is:
A. Symmetrical
B. Skewed to the left
C. Skewed to the right
D. Bimodal
**Answer**: C

#### Mean And Median In Perfectly Symmetrical Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a perfectly symmetrical distribution, the mean and the median are ___.
**Answer**: the same

#### Mean And Median In Perfectly Symmetrical Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a perfectly symmetrical distribution, which relationship holds?
A. Mean equals median
B. Mean is less than median
C. Mean is greater than median
D. Mean equals mode but not median
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Versus Median Sensitivity To Skewness (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Both the mean and the median reflect skewing, but the ___ reflects it more.
**Answer**: mean

#### Mean Versus Median Sensitivity To Skewness (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When a distribution is skewed, which measure tends to move farther toward the tail?
A. Mean
B. Median
C. Mode
D. They reflect it equally
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Order In Left-Skewed Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a left-skewed distribution, the typical order is mean < median < ___.
**Answer**: mode

#### Relative Order In Left-Skewed Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which ordering matches a left-skewed distribution?
A. mean < median < mode
B. mean < mode < median
C. mode < median < mean
D. median < mean < mode
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Order In Right-Skewed Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a right-skewed distribution, ___ < median < mean.
**Answer**: mode

#### Relative Order In Right-Skewed Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which ordering matches a right-skewed distribution?
A. mode < median < mean
B. mean < median < mode
C. mean < mode < median
D. median < mode < mean
**Answer**: A

#### Median And Mean Positions Relative To Mode In Skewed Distributions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In skewed distributions, the ___ is closest to the high point (the mode).
**Answer**: median

#### Median And Mean Positions Relative To Mode In Skewed Distributions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a skewed distribution, which measure tends to be farthest out on the tail?
A. Mean
B. Median
C. Mode
D. All are equally far
**Answer**: A

#### Mean And Median Location In Symmetrical Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a symmetrical distribution, the mean and the median are centrally located close to the ___ of the distribution.
**Answer**: high point

#### Mean And Median Location In Symmetrical Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a symmetrical distribution like Maris’ data, where are the mean and median relative to the shape?
A. Centrally located near the high point
B. Far out on the left tail
C. Far out on the right tail
D. Midway between tails but away from the high point
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_31

#### Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard deviation is always ___ or zero.
**Answer**: positive

#### Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes the standard deviation?
A. It measures how far data values are from the mean.
B. It measures the center of the data.
C. It measures the skewness of the data.
D. It is always negative or zero.
**Answer**: A

#### Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a population value x, the deviation is ___.
**Answer**: x - μ

#### Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a sample, the deviation of a value x from the mean is:
A. x - x̄
B. x + x̄
C. μ - x
D. σ - x̄
**Answer**: A

#### Variance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The variance is the average of the ___ deviations.
**Answer**: squared

#### Variance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about variance and standard deviation?
A. The standard deviation is the square root of the variance.
B. The variance is the square root of the standard deviation.
C. The variance equals the mean.
D. The variance has the same units as the data.
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample standard deviation is denoted ___.
**Answer**: s

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When computing a sample variance/standard deviation, the denominator is:
A. n - 1
B. n
C. N - 1
D. N
**Answer**: A

#### Population Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The population standard deviation uses denominator ___ when computing the variance.
**Answer**: N

#### Population Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The symbol for the population standard deviation is:
A. σ
B. s
C. μ
D. x̄
**Answer**: A

#### Number Of Standard Deviations (#ofSTDEVs) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The relationship value = mean + (___)(standard deviation) expresses how far a value is from the mean.
**Answer**: #ofSTDEVs

#### Number Of Standard Deviations (#ofSTDEVs) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the mean is 5 and the standard deviation is 2, what is the # of STDEVs for the value 1?
A. -2
B. 2
C. -1
D. 4
**Answer**: A

#### Z-Score (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a sample, z = ___.
**Answer**: (x - x̄)/s

#### Z-Score (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a population with mean 80 and standard deviation 10, the z-score for x = 77 is:
A. -0.3
B. 0.3
C. -3
D. 3
**Answer**: A

#### Grouped Data Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For grouped frequency data, we use ___ to estimate the standard deviation because individual values are unknown.
**Answer**: interval midpoints

#### Grouped Data Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about the standard deviation for grouped data?
A. It is an estimate computed using class midpoints.
B. It equals the exact standard deviation of the raw data.
C. It is computed from individual raw data values.
D. It cannot be computed in any form.
**Answer**: A

#### Variability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Describing data in terms of its spread is called ___.
**Answer**: variability

#### Variability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about variability and standard deviation is correct?
A. If the standard deviation is zero, all data values are equal.
B. A large standard deviation means values are tightly clustered near the mean.
C. Variability is unrelated to how the data were obtained.
D. A standard deviation can be negative.
**Answer**: A

#### Chebyshev's Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Chebyshev's Rule states that at least ___% of data lie within two standard deviations of the mean.
**Answer**: 75%

#### Chebyshev's Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is guaranteed by Chebyshev’s Rule for any data set?
A. At least 89% of data lie within three standard deviations of the mean.
B. Approximately 68% of data lie within one standard deviation of the mean.
C. More than 99% of data lie within three standard deviations of the mean.
D. Exactly 95% of data lie within two standard deviations of the mean.
**Answer**: A

#### Empirical Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The Empirical Rule applies only to distributions that are ___ and ___.
**Answer**: bell-shaped and symmetric

#### Empirical Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: According to the Empirical Rule, about what percent of data lie within two standard deviations of the mean in a bell-shaped, symmetric distribution?
A. 95%
B. 68%
C. 75%
D. 89%
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_33

#### Box Plot (Q1)

**Answer**: box plot

#### Box Plot (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which numbers do you need to draw a box plot?
A. minimum, Q1, median, Q3, maximum
B. mean and standard deviation
C. mode and range
D. only the quartiles
**Answer**: A

#### First Quartile (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The first quartile (Q1) is the ___ of the lower half of the ordered data set.
**Answer**: median

#### First Quartile (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Q1 corresponds to which percentile?
A. 25th percentile
B. 50th percentile
C. 75th percentile
D. 10th percentile
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a data set, ___ is the number of times a value occurs.
**Answer**: frequency

#### Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If 14 people report selling three cars in a week, the frequency for "3 cars" is:
A. 14
B. 3
C. 17
D. 65
**Answer**: A

#### Frequency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ displays grouped data along with the corresponding counts.
**Answer**: frequency table

#### Frequency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which display lists class intervals with how many observations fall in each?
A. Frequency table
B. Line graph
C. Stem-and-leaf plot
D. Box plot
**Answer**: A

#### Histogram (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a histogram, the vertical axis shows the ___.
**Answer**: frequency

#### Histogram (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which graph is typically used for large, continuous, quantitative data sets?
A. Histogram
B. Bar graph
C. Box plot
D. Stem-and-leaf plot
**Answer**: A

#### Interquartile Range (IQR) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: IQR = ___ − ___.
**Answer**: Q3 − Q1

#### Interquartile Range (IQR) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The IQR covers what percent of the data?
A. 50%
B. 25%
C. 75%
D. 100%
**Answer**: A

#### Interval (Class Interval) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An ___ represents a range of data used when displaying large data sets.
**Answer**: interval

#### Interval (Class Interval) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a table with classes like 49.5–59.5, each class is called a:
A. Interval (class interval)
B. Midpoint
C. Quartile
D. Percentile
**Answer**: A

#### Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample mean x̄ equals the ___ of the sample values divided by the number of values.
**Answer**: sum

#### Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A common name for the mean is:
A. average
B. midpoint
C. mode
D. median
**Answer**: A

#### Median (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The median may or may not be part of the ___.
**Answer**: data

#### Median (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The median corresponds to which percentile?
A. 50th percentile
B. 25th percentile
C. 75th percentile
D. 60th percentile
**Answer**: A

#### Outlier (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An ___ is an observation that does not fit the rest of the data.
**Answer**: outlier

#### Outlier (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A value above Q3 + 1.5(IQR) is flagged as a:
A. potential outlier
B. median
C. quartile
D. mode
**Answer**: A

#### Percentile (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The first quartile (Q1) is the ___ percentile.
**Answer**: 25th

#### Percentile (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: An observation at the 50th percentile is greater than what percent of the other observations?
A. 50%
B. 25%
C. 75%
D. It cannot be determined
**Answer**: A

#### Quartiles (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The second quartile is the ___.
**Answer**: median

#### Quartiles (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Quartiles divide data into how many equal parts?
A. four
B. two
C. three
D. ten
**Answer**: A

#### Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Relative frequency is the ratio of a class's frequency to the ___ number of outcomes.
**Answer**: total

#### Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a completed frequency table, the relative frequencies should sum to approximately:
A. 1 (or 100%)
B. 0
C. the total number of outcomes
D. the largest single frequency
**Answer**: A

#### Skewed (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Data with more spread among greater values are skewed to the ___.
**Answer**: right

#### Skewed (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When the right side of a graph looks “chopped off” compared to the left, the data are skewed to the:
A. left
B. right
C. center
D. neither
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Standard deviation measures how far data values are from their ___.
**Answer**: mean

#### Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which symbol denotes the population standard deviation?
A. σ
B. s
C. μ
D. x̄
**Answer**: A

#### IQR Outlier Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A potential high outlier is above ___ + 1.5(IQR).
**Answer**: Q3

#### IQR Outlier Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: According to the IQR rule, a value below Q1 − 1.5(IQR) is:
A. a potential outlier
B. the median
C. an interval midpoint
D. the mode
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a sample, s = sqrt[∑(x − x̄)² / (___ − 1)].
**Answer**: n

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The sample standard deviation uses which denominator?
A. n − 1
B. n
C. N
D. N − 2
**Answer**: A

#### Population Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a population, σ = sqrt[∑(x − µ)² / ___].
**Answer**: N

#### Population Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When computing a population standard deviation, you divide by:
A. N
B. n − 1
C. N − 1
D. n
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_37

#### Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: _____ is a measure of how certain we are of outcomes of an experiment or activity.
**Answer**: Probability

#### Probability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If P(A) = 1, what does this mean about event A?
A. A. It can never happen
B. B. It always happens
C. C. It is equally likely to happen or not
D. D. The sample space is empty
**Answer**: B

#### Experiment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An _____ is a planned operation carried out under controlled conditions.
**Answer**: experiment

#### Experiment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which of the following is an experiment described in the section?
A. A. Flipping one fair coin twice
B. B. Getting a head (H)
C. C. S = {H, T}
D. D. Event A = getting at most one head
**Answer**: A

#### Chance Experiment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If the result is not predetermined, the experiment is called a _____.
**Answer**: chance experiment

#### Chance Experiment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which activity is a chance experiment?
A. A. Rolling a fair six-sided die
B. B. Listing a sample space
C. C. Computing P(A)
D. D. Naming event B
**Answer**: A

#### Outcome (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A result of an experiment is called an _____.
**Answer**: outcome

#### Outcome (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When you flip one fair coin, which is an outcome?
A. A. H
B. B. {H, T}
C. C. P(H) = 0.5
D. D. Event A = getting at most one head
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Space (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The set of all possible outcomes is the _____, denoted by S.
**Answer**: sample space

#### Sample Space (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is S when flipping one fair coin once?
A. A. {H, T}
B. B. {HH, HT, TH, TT}
C. C. {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}
D. D. {2, 4, 6}
**Answer**: A

#### Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An _____ is any combination of outcomes, often denoted by A or B.
**Answer**: event

#### Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an example of an event from the section?
A. A. Getting at most one head when flipping one fair coin
B. B. S = {H, T}
C. C. H (heads)
D. D. The set {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}
**Answer**: A

#### Probability Range (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Probabilities are between ___ and ___, inclusive.
**Answer**: 0 and 1

#### Probability Range (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which value cannot be a probability?
A. A. -0.2
B. B. 0
C. C. 0.5
D. D. 1
**Answer**: A

#### Equally Likely Outcomes (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When each outcome occurs with the same probability, the outcomes are _____.
**Answer**: equally likely

#### Equally Likely Outcomes (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation has equally likely outcomes according to the section?
A. A. Tossing a fair six-sided die
B. B. Flipping a coin that showed heads 56% of the time in 250 trials
C. C. Using dice with holes that change face weights
D. D. Selecting only the numbers 5 or 6
**Answer**: A

#### Probability For Equally Likely Outcomes (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For equally likely outcomes, P(A) equals the number of outcomes in A divided by the number of outcomes in the ___.
**Answer**: sample space

#### Probability For Equally Likely Outcomes (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: You toss a fair dime and a fair nickel. What is P(getting one head)?
A. A. 0
B. B. 0.25
C. C. 0.5
D. D. 0.75
**Answer**: C

#### Long-Term Relative Frequency (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The probability of an outcome can be interpreted as its long-run _____ frequency.
**Answer**: relative

#### Long-Term Relative Frequency (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If you flip a fair coin many times, the fraction of heads approaches which value?
A. A. 0.5
B. B. 0
C. C. 1
D. D. 2
**Answer**: A

#### Law Of Large Numbers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The principle that observed relative frequency approaches theoretical probability as repetitions increase is the _____.
**Answer**: law of large numbers

#### Law Of Large Numbers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Rolling a fair die many times, about what fraction of rolls would be at least five in the long run?
A. A. 1/6
B. B. 2/6
C. C. 3/6
D. D. 5/6
**Answer**: B

#### Biased (Unfair) Outcomes (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When outcomes are not equally likely due to bias (for example, heads 56% of the time), the outcomes are called _____.
**Answer**: biased

#### Biased (Unfair) Outcomes (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In 250 trials of a Belgian one Euro coin, heads occurred 56% of the time. What does this suggest?
A. A. The coin may be biased
B. B. The coin is certainly fair
C. C. Outcomes are equally likely
D. D. P(H) = 0
**Answer**: A

#### Or Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An outcome is in A OR B if it is in A, in B, or in _____.
**Answer**: both

#### Or Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5} and B = {4, 5, 6, 7, 8}, what is A OR B?
A. A. {4, 5}
B. B. {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8}
C. C. {1, 2, 3}
D. D. {6, 7, 8}
**Answer**: B

#### And Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5} and B = {4, 5, 6, 7, 8}, A AND B = _____.
**Answer**: {4, 5}

#### And Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using A = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5} and B = {4, 5, 6, 7, 8}, which set equals A AND B?
A. A. {4, 5}
B. B. {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8}
C. C. {1, 2, 3}
D. D. {6, 7, 8}
**Answer**: A

#### Complement Of An Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The complement of event A, denoted A′, consists of all outcomes that are ___ in A.
**Answer**: not in A

#### Complement Of An Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If S = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6} and A = {1, 2, 3, 4}, what is A′?
A. A. {5, 6}
B. B. {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}
C. C. {1, 2, 3, 4}
D. D. {4, 5}
**Answer**: A

#### Complement Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The complement rule states P(A) + P(A′) = ___.
**Answer**: 1

#### Complement Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If S = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6} and P(A) = 4/6, what is P(A′)?
A. A. 2/6
B. B. 4/6
C. C. 6/6
D. D. 0
**Answer**: A

#### Conditional Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Conditional probability is written P(A|B) and equals P(A AND B) divided by ___ (with P(B) > 0).
**Answer**: P(B)

#### Conditional Probability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With S = {1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6}, A = {2, 3} and B = {2, 4, 6}, what is P(A|B)?
A. A. 1/6
B. B. 1/3
C. C. 2/3
D. D. 1
**Answer**: B
