## Section: seg_39

#### Independent Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Two events A and B are independent if ___.
**Answer**: P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B)

#### Independent Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an example of independent events?
A. Two rolls of a fair die
B. Drawing two cards from a 52-card deck without replacement
C. Picking three cards from a 52-card deck without replacement
D. Drawing two cards from a deck and not putting the first back before the second draw
**Answer**: A

#### Dependent Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If two events are not independent, they are called ___ events.
**Answer**: dependent

#### Dependent Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When drawing two cards from a 52-card deck without replacement, the draws are:
A. Independent
B. Dependent
C. Mutually exclusive
D. Complements
**Answer**: B

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Sampling ___ replacement allows the same card to be chosen more than once.
**Answer**: with

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which outcome can only occur when sampling with replacement?
A. QS, 1D, 1C, QD
B. KH, 7D, 6D, KH
C. QS, 7D, 6D, KS
D. K of hearts, three of diamonds, J of spades
**Answer**: B

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When sampling ___ replacement, each member can be chosen only once.
**Answer**: without

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: You draw three cards and cannot pick the same card twice. What sampling method was used?
A. With replacement
B. Without replacement
C. Independent
D. Mutually exclusive
**Answer**: B

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Events A and B are mutually exclusive if ___.
**Answer**: P(A AND B) = 0

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When two fair coins are flipped, the events “all heads” and “all tails” are:
A. Independent
B. Dependent
C. Mutually exclusive
D. Complements
**Answer**: C

#### Showing Independence (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To show two events are independent, you need to verify only ___ of the standard conditions.
**Answer**: one

#### Showing Independence (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement, if true, is sufficient to show that A and B are independent?
A. P(A AND B) = 0
B. P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B)
C. P(A) = P(B)
D. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B)
**Answer**: B

## Section: seg_41

#### Multiplication Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In Carlos’s soccer example, the probability that he makes both goals is ___.
**Answer**: 0.585

#### Multiplication Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula is the Multiplication Rule for any events A and B?
A. P(A AND B) = P(B)P(A|B)
B. P(A AND B) = P(A) + P(B)
C. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B) - P(A AND B)
D. P(A|B) = P(A AND B) / P(B)
**Answer**: A

#### Conditional Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: By definition, P(A|B) = ___ / P(B).
**Answer**: P(A AND B)

#### Conditional Probability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Felicity’s class-enrollment example, with P(M|S) = 0.25 and P(S) = 0.65, what is P(M AND S)?
A. 0.1625
B. 0.40
C. 0.15
D. 0.90
**Answer**: A

#### Addition Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Klaus can only afford one vacation. The probability he chooses New Zealand or Alaska is ___.
**Answer**: 0.95

#### Addition Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Carlos’s example, what is P(A OR B) for making at least one of the two goals?
A. 0.585
B. 0.715
C. 1.235
D. 0.065
**Answer**: B

#### Independent Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If A and B are independent, then P(A AND B) = ___.
**Answer**: P(A)P(B)

#### Independent Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Are having breast cancer (B) and testing negative (N) independent in Example 3.18?
A. Yes, because P(N|B) = P(N)
B. No, because P(N|B) ≠ P(N)
C. Yes, because P(B AND N) = 0
D. No, because P(B AND N) = P(B)P(N)
**Answer**: B

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If A and B are mutually exclusive, then P(A AND B) = ___.
**Answer**: 0

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For Klaus’s vacation choices A = New Zealand and B = Alaska, which statement is true?
A. A and B are mutually exclusive because P(A AND B) = 0
B. A and B are independent because P(A|B) = P(A)
C. A and B are both independent and mutually exclusive
D. A and B are neither independent nor mutually exclusive
**Answer**: A

D. 59/100

B. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B)

C. P(A|B) = P(A)P(B)

## Section: seg_45

#### Tree Diagram (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ is a special graph used to determine outcomes of an experiment, with branches labeled by frequencies or probabilities.
**Answer**: tree diagram

**Multiple Choice**: What do tree diagrams help you do in probability problems?
A. visualize outcomes and solve probability problems by branching with frequencies or probabilities
B. avoid using probabilities altogether
C. ensure events are independent
D. eliminate the need for sample spaces
**Answer**: A

#### With Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: With replacement means you ___ the first selection before making the second.
**Answer**: return the first selection to the pool

#### With Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: An urn has 11 balls. Drawing two balls with replacement, how many ordered outcomes are possible?
A. 121
B. 110
C. 22
D. 33
**Answer**: A

#### Without Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Without replacement means you ___ the first selection before making the second.
**Answer**: do not return the first selection to the pool

#### Without Replacement (Q2)

A. 3/10
B. 3/11
C. 8/10
D. 8/11
**Answer**: A

#### Branch Multiplication In Tree Diagrams (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a probability-labeled tree, the outcome value is found by ___ the probabilities on the corresponding branches.
**Answer**: multiplying

A. 6/110
B. 9/121
C. 3/11
D. 5/21
**Answer**: A

#### Conditional Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A conditional probability is evaluated on a ___ sample space where the given event holds.
**Answer**: reduced

#### Conditional Probability (Q2)

A. 3/11
B. 3/10
C. 8/11
D. 24/121
**Answer**: A

**Fill-in-the-blank**: P(A|B) denotes the probability of ___.
**Answer**: event A given event B

#### Conditional Probability Notation (Q2)

A. P(B on 2nd|R on 1st)
B. P(R on 1st|B on 2nd)
C. P(B on 2nd AND R on 1st)
D. P(B on 2nd OR R on 1st)
**Answer**: A

#### Venn Diagram (Q1)

#### Venn Diagram (Q2)

A. the rectangle
B. the overlapping region
C. each circle
D. the area outside the rectangle
**Answer**: A

**Fill-in-the-blank**: In Example 3.27, A AND B = ___.

B. TH
C. HT
D. HH

#### Event Union (OR) (Q1)

## Section: seg_47

#### Conditional Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: P(A|B) is the probability that event A occurs given that ___
**Answer**: event B has already occurred

A. P(A OR B)
B. P(A AND B)
C. P(B|A)
D. P(A|B)
**Answer**: D

#### Dependent Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If two events are not independent, then they are called ___
**Answer**: dependent events

#### Dependent Events (Q2)

A. Independent
B. Dependent
C. Mutually exclusive
D. Equally likely
**Answer**: B

#### Independent Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Events A and B are independent if P(A AND B) = ___
**Answer**: P(A)P(B)

#### Independent Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement shows that events A and B are independent?
A. P(A|B) = P(A)
B. P(B|A) = P(B)
C. P(A AND B) = P(A)P(B)
D. All of the above
**Answer**: D

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If events A and B are mutually exclusive, then P(A AND B) = ___
**Answer**: 0

#### Mutually Exclusive Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If A and B are mutually exclusive, which must be true?
A. P(A AND B) = 0
B. P(A) = 0
C. P(B) = 1
D. P(A OR B) = P(A)P(B)
**Answer**: A

#### Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For the sample space S, the axioms state that ___
**Answer**: P(S) = 1

#### Probability (Q2)

A. P(A) ≥ 1
B. P(A) ≤ 0
C. 0 ≤ P(A) ≤ 1
D. P(A) = 1 − P(S)
**Answer**: C

#### Sample Space (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The set of all possible outcomes of an experiment is called the ___
**Answer**: sample space

**Multiple Choice**: In a Venn diagram, the box represents the:
A. Event A
B. Complement of A
C. Sample space
D. Intersection of A and B
**Answer**: C

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In sampling with replacement, after a member is picked, you ___ it before the next pick
**Answer**: replace it

#### Sampling With Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With replacement, the events on successive picks are:
A. Independent
B. Dependent
C. Mutually exclusive
D. Impossible to compare
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In sampling without replacement, each member may be chosen only ___
**Answer**: once

#### Sampling Without Replacement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Without replacement, the events on successive picks are considered:
A. Independent
B. Not independent
C. Mutually exclusive
D. Equally likely
**Answer**: B

#### And Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An outcome is in A AND B if it is in ___ at the same time
**Answer**: both A and B

#### And Event (Q2)

A. P(A OR B)
B. P(A AND B)
C. P(A')
D. P(B')
**Answer**: B

#### Complement Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The complement of event A consists of all outcomes that are ___
**Answer**: not in A

#### Complement Event (Q2)

A. 0.30
B. 0.70
C. 1.30
D. -0.30
**Answer**: B

#### Or Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An outcome is in A OR B if it is in A, in B, or in ___
**Answer**: both A and B

#### Or Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the event A OR B?
A. Outcomes only in A
B. Outcomes only in B
C. Outcomes in neither A nor B
D. Outcomes in A, in B, or in both
**Answer**: D

#### Multiplication Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The multiplication rule states P(A AND B) = ___
**Answer**: P(A|B)P(B)

#### Multiplication Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula is the multiplication rule?
A. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B) − P(A AND B)
B. P(A AND B) = P(A|B)P(B)
C. P(S) = 1
D. P(A') = 1 − P(A)
**Answer**: B

#### Addition Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The addition rule states P(A OR B) = ___
**Answer**: P(A) + P(B) − P(A AND B)

#### Addition Rule (Q2)

A. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B)
B. P(A OR B) = P(A)P(B)
C. P(A OR B) = P(A) + P(B) − 1
D. P(A OR B) = 0
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: μ

A. 0.9
B. 1.1
C. 1.5
D. 2.0
**Answer**: B

**Answer**: x*P(x)

D. x, frequency, and relative frequency
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: A

D. 0.3

A. 4.838
B. 8.2
C. 2.20
D. 0.41

#### Binomcdf (Q2)

A. 2
B. 49.5
C. 50
D. 2450
**Answer**: C

#### Variance Of Geometric Distribution (Q1)

**Answer**: (1/p)(1/p − 1)

A. 50
B. 49.5
C. 2450
D. 2500
**Answer**: C

#### Standard Deviation Of Geometric Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For X ~ G(p), the standard deviation is σ = ___.
**Answer**: sqrt((1/p)(1/p − 1))

#### Standard Deviation Of Geometric Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For p = 0.02, what is the standard deviation of X?
A. 50
B. 49.5
C. 2450
D. 0.02
**Answer**: B

#### Poisson Distribution (Q1)

**Answer**: interval of time or space

#### Poisson Distribution (Q2)

B. Counting successes in n trials with success probability p.

D. Counting events in a fixed interval when the average rate changes during the interval.
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: one broadcast

**Answer**: A

**Answer**: the number of times the news reporter says "uh" during one broadcast

**Answer**: A

**Answer**: independent

**Answer**: 3, 1/6

D. (n choose x) μ^x q^(n − x)

**Answer**: the sum of

#### Uniform Distribution (Q1)

A. X ~ U(0, 23)

**Answer**: A

B. 1/14

D. 15

B. The probability that X is less than or equal to x.

D. The area between x and the maximum value.
**Answer**: B

#### Uniform Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For X ~ U(a, b), the pdf equals ___ for a <= x <= b.
**Answer**: 1/(b - a)

#### Uniform Distribution (Q2)

A. 0.25
B. 0.375
C. 0.50
D. 0.625
**Answer**: B

#### Exponential Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For X ~ Exp(m), the cdf is F(x) = ___ for x >= 0.
**Answer**: 1 - e^(-m x)

#### Exponential Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which feature is characteristic of the exponential distribution?
A. Memoryless property
B. Symmetric, bell-shaped curve
C. Finite support between a and b
D. Mean equals zero
**Answer**: A

#### Decay Parameter (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For an exponential distribution with mean mu, the decay parameter m equals ___.
**Answer**: 1/mu

#### Decay Parameter (Q2)

A. 5
B. 0.2
C. e^(-5)
D. 2
**Answer**: B

#### Conditional Probability (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Conditional probability is the likelihood of an event occurring given that another event has already ___.
**Answer**: occurred

#### Conditional Probability (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In P(X > 7 | X > 4), the symbol '|' means:
A. and
B. or
C. given that
D. equals
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_81

#### Area To The Left (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a normal distribution, ___ equals the area to the left of the vertical line at x.
**Answer**: P(X < x)

**Multiple Choice**: Which expression represents the area to the left of x?
A. P(X < x)
B. P(X > x)
C. 1 − P(X < x)
D. P(X ≥ x)
**Answer**: A

#### Area To The Right (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The area to the right of x in a normal distribution is ___.
**Answer**: 1 − P(X < x)

B. 0.5000
C. 0.9772
D. 0.6554

**Answer**: P(X ≤ x)

D. P(X = x) > 0

## Section: seg_97

#### Central Limit Theorem (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: According to the central limit theorem, as n becomes large, the distribution of sample means approaches a ___.
**Answer**: normal distribution

#### Central Limit Theorem (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For large n from a population with mean μ and standard deviation σ, which is the correct distribution for the sample sum ΣX?
A. N(nμ, √n·σ)
B. N(μ, σ/√n)
C. N(μ, σ)
D. N(nμ, nσ)
**Answer**: A

#### Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means (Averages) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Under the CLT for sample means, X̄ ~ N(μ, ___) for large n.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### Central Limit Theorem For Sample Means (Averages) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For large n, what is the mean of the sampling distribution of X̄?
A. μ
B. μ/n
C. nμ
D. 0
**Answer**: A

#### Central Limit Theorem For Sums (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For large n, the standard deviation of the distribution of the sums ΣX is ___.
**Answer**: √n·σ

#### Central Limit Theorem For Sums (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is true about the distribution of ΣX for large n, regardless of the population's shape?
A. It is approximately normal with mean nμ.
B. It is uniform with mean μ.
C. It remains skewed with mean μ/n.
D. It is exponential with mean 1/μ.
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The probability distribution of a statistic computed from all simple random samples of size n is called a ___.
**Answer**: sampling distribution

#### Sampling Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which of the following is a sampling distribution?
A. The distribution of sample means from many samples
B. The histogram of a single sample
C. The pdf of a uniform population
D. The cdf of a normal population
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Error Of The Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard error of the mean equals ___.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### Standard Error Of The Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: As n increases, the standard error of the mean...
A. decreases
B. stays the same
C. increases linearly
D. doubles
**Answer**: A

#### Normal Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the notation X ~ N(μ, σ), σ represents the ___ of the distribution.
**Answer**: standard deviation

#### Normal Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula gives the pdf of a normal distribution?
A. f(x) = (1/(σ√(2π))) e^{-(x−μ)^2/(2σ^2)}
B. f(x) = (1/(b−a)) for a < x < b
C. f(x) = me^{−mx}, x ≥ 0
D. f(x) = (x−μ)^2/(2σ^2)
**Answer**: A

#### Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The population mean μ is (sum of all values in the population) divided by the ___.
**Answer**: number of values in the population

#### Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which term is another common name for the arithmetic mean?
A. average
B. median
C. mode
D. range
**Answer**: A

#### Z-Score For Sample Means (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The z-score for a sample mean is z = (x̄ − μx)/(___).
**Answer**: σx/√n

#### Z-Score For Sample Means (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Given μ = 10, σ = 2, n = 25, and x̄ = 10.8, what is the z-score for the sample mean?
A. 2.0
B. 0.2
C. 4.0
D. −2.0
**Answer**: A

#### Z-Score For Sums (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The z-score for a sample sum is z = (Σx − nμx)/(___).
**Answer**: √n·σx

#### Z-Score For Sums (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For μ = 80, σ = 12, n = 95, which standard deviation should be used when standardizing ΣX?
A. √n·σ = √95·12
B. σ/√n = 12/√95
C. σ = 12
D. nσ = 95·12
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_101

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The confidence interval estimate for μ has the format (x̄ − EBM, ___).
**Answer**: x̄ + EBM

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With x̄ = 7 and EBM = 2.5, what is the confidence interval estimate for μ?
A. (4.5, 9.5)
B. (2.5, 7)
C. (5, 15)
D. (7, 9.5)
**Answer**: A

#### Point Estimate Of The Population Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The point estimate of the unknown population mean μ is the ___.
**Answer**: sample mean x̄

#### Point Estimate Of The Population Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In constructing a z-interval for μ when σ is known, which quantity is used as the point estimate?
A. Population mean μ
B. Sample mean x̄
C. Population standard deviation σ
D. z-score z_{α/2}
**Answer**: B

#### Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a known σ, the error bound for a mean is EBM = ___ · (σ/√n).
**Answer**: z_{α/2}

#### Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With σ and n unchanged, which change increases EBM?
A. Increase CL from 90% to 95%
B. Decrease CL from 95% to 90%
C. Increase n
D. Use a smaller σ
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Level (CL) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Numerically, the confidence level satisfies CL = 1 − ___.
**Answer**: α

#### Confidence Level (CL) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a 90% confidence interval, what are α and the area in each tail?
A. α = 0.10; 0.05 in each tail
B. α = 0.05; 0.025 in each tail
C. α = 0.90; 0.45 in each tail
D. α = 0.10; 0.10 in each tail
**Answer**: A

#### Alpha (α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: α is the probability that the confidence interval ___ the true parameter.
**Answer**: does not contain

#### Alpha (α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If CL = 0.95, what is α/2?
A. 0.025
B. 0.05
C. 0.95
D. 0.475
**Answer**: A

#### Z-Score For Confidence Level (z_{α/2}) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For CL = 95%, z_{α/2} = ___.
**Answer**: 1.96

#### Z-Score For Confidence Level (z_{α/2}) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which z-value puts 0.90 in the center of the standard normal (0.05 in each tail)?
A. 1.28
B. 1.645
C. 1.96
D. 2.326
**Answer**: B

#### Standard Error Of The Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard error of the mean equals ___.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### Standard Error Of The Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If σ = 3 and n = 36, what is the standard error of x̄?
A. 0.5
B. 3
C. 6
D. 0.8225
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When σ is known, the sampling distribution of x̄ is approximately normal with mean ___ and standard deviation σ/√n.
**Answer**: μ

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement justifies using the normal distribution for intervals when σ is known?
A. x̄ is approximately normally distributed with mean μ and standard deviation σ/√n
B. The population is always normal regardless of n
C. Individual observations have standard deviation σ/√n
D. μ is normally distributed with mean x̄
**Answer**: A

#### Conditions For Using A Z-Interval For A Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To use a z-interval for a mean, we require a ___ sample and a known σ.
**Answer**: random

#### Conditions For Using A Z-Interval For A Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation meets the conditions for a z-interval for a mean?
A. A random sample with known population σ
B. A convenience sample with unknown σ
C. A random sample with unknown σ
D. A census with unknown σ
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Interval Construction Steps (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: After computing x̄ and finding z for the stated CL, the next step is to calculate the ___.
**Answer**: EBM

#### Confidence Interval Construction Steps (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which sequence is correct for constructing a z-interval for μ?
A. Compute x̄; find z; compute EBM; form x̄ ± EBM; interpret
B. Find z; interpret; compute x̄; compute EBM; form interval
C. Compute EBM; compute x̄; find z; interpret; form interval
D. Interpret; compute x̄; form interval; find z; compute EBM
**Answer**: A

#### Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A proper interpretation should state the ___, identify μ, and give the interval.
**Answer**: confidence level

#### Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Given the 90% CI (67.18, 68.82) for a mean exam score, which interpretation is appropriate?
A. We estimate with 90% confidence that the true mean score is between 67.18 and 68.82
B. There is a 90% probability that μ equals 68
C. About 90% of student scores fall between 67.18 and 68.82
D. Exactly 90% of sample means will lie in this single interval
**Answer**: A

#### Effect Of Changing The Confidence Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Increasing the confidence level ___ the error bound, making the interval wider.
**Answer**: increases

#### Effect Of Changing The Confidence Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Compared to a 90% CI, a 95% CI based on the same data will have:
A. A larger EBM and be wider
B. The same EBM and same width
C. A smaller EBM and be narrower
D. No change in EBM but a wider interval
**Answer**: A

#### Effect Of Changing The Sample Size (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Increasing n ___ the error bound, making the confidence interval narrower.
**Answer**: decreases

#### Effect Of Changing The Sample Size (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using σ = 3 and CL = 90%, which sample size gives the largest EBM?
A. n = 100
B. n = 36
C. n = 25
D. All give the same EBM
**Answer**: C

#### Working Backwards From A Confidence Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Given a CI (L, U), the error bound is ___.
**Answer**: (U − L)/2

#### Working Backwards From A Confidence Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If a CI is (67.18, 68.82), what is the sample mean x̄?
A. 67.18
B. 68
C. 0.82
D. 68.82
**Answer**: B

#### Sample Size For A Desired Margin Of Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To achieve margin EBM with known σ, the required sample size is n = ___ (using z = z_{α/2}).
**Answer**: z^2 σ^2 / EBM^2

#### Sample Size For A Desired Margin Of Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If σ = 15, CL = 95%, and desired EBM = 2, what sample size is required?
A. 196
B. 200
C. 217
D. 225
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_103

#### Student's T-Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Compared with the standard normal distribution, the Student's t-distribution has ___ in its tails.
**Answer**: more probability in its tails

#### Student's T-Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which feature distinguishes the Student's t-distribution from the standard normal distribution?
A. Heavier tails and greater spread
B. Mean not equal to zero
C. Same shape regardless of degrees of freedom
D. Becomes less like normal as degrees of freedom increase
**Answer**: A

#### When To Use Student's T-Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When the population standard deviation σ is unknown and s is used as an estimate, you should use the ___ distribution.
**Answer**: Student's t-

#### When To Use Student's T-Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Today’s recommended practice is to use the Student’s t-distribution when:
A. σ is unknown and s is used as an estimate
B. The sample size is larger than 30
C. The population is heavily non-normal
D. σ is known
**Answer**: A

#### T-Score (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The t-score for a sample mean is t = ___.
**Answer**: (x̄ − μ) / (s / √n)

#### T-Score (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Under the stated assumptions, t-scores follow which distribution?
A. Student’s t with n − 1 degrees of freedom
B. Standard normal for all n
C. Student’s t with n degrees of freedom
D. Uniform distribution on (−1, 1)
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a sample of size n, the degrees of freedom used with the t-distribution is ___.
**Answer**: n − 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why is df = n − 1 when computing the sample standard deviation s?
A. The last deviation is determined by the first n − 1 because their sum must be zero
B. We drop the smallest observation by convention
C. Because μ is unknown
D. We always round the sample size down by one
**Answer**: A

#### Properties Of The Student's T-Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The mean of the Student's t-distribution is ___.
**Answer**: zero

#### Properties Of The Student's T-Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: As the degrees of freedom increase, the Student's t-distribution becomes:
A. More like the standard normal distribution
B. Less symmetric
C. Shifted so the mean is not zero
D. Uniform over all real numbers
**Answer**: A

#### Assumptions For Using Student's T-Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To use the Student’s t-distribution for a single mean, take a ___ from a population that is approximately normal.
**Answer**: simple random sample

#### Assumptions For Using Student's T-Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which set of conditions matches the assumptions for using the t-distribution here?
A. Simple random sample; population approximately normal; μ and σ unknown
B. Convenience sample; large population; μ known
C. Simple random sample; population heavily skewed; σ known
D. Nonrandom sample; population normal; σ known
**Answer**: A

#### Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When σ is unknown, the error bound for the mean is EBM = ___.
**Answer**: t_(α/2) × s / √n

#### Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In EBM = t_(α/2) × s/√n, t_(α/2) is:
A. The t-score with right-tail area α/2
B. The z-score with left-tail area α/2
C. The t-score with left-tail area α/2
D. The t-score with right-tail area α
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Mean (σ Unknown) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The confidence interval format for μ when σ is unknown is (___, ___).
**Answer**: x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Mean (σ Unknown) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: On a TI-84+, which menu item computes a t-interval directly?
A. 8:TInterval
B. 7:ZInterval
C. invT on the DISTRibution menu
D. 1-PropZInt
**Answer**: A

#### Student's T Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Student’s t table gives t-scores for a given degrees of freedom and the ___.
**Answer**: right-tailed probability

#### Student's T Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When using a Student’s t table:
A. Some tables show confidence levels in the columns, others show tail areas
B. All t tables list only left-tail areas
C. t tables provide probabilities but not t-scores
D. t tables are unnecessary because the TI-83 has invT
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_105

#### Population Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The true proportion of successes in a population is denoted by ___.
**Answer**: p

#### Population Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes the population proportion in a binomial context?
A. The long-run fraction of successes in the population.
B. The sample fraction of successes observed.
C. The number of successes in the sample.
D. The mean of a normal distribution.
**Answer**: A

#### Binomial Distribution For Counts (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When counting successes in n independent trials with success probability p, we model X as ___.
**Answer**: B(n, p)

#### Binomial Distribution For Counts (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In this section, the underlying distribution for counts of successes was identified as:
A. Poisson with mean p
B. Binomial with parameters n and p
C. Normal with mean p and variance pq
D. t-distribution with n−1 degrees of freedom
**Answer**: B

#### Sample Proportion (p′ Or p̂) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample proportion p′ is computed as ___.
**Answer**: X/n

#### Sample Proportion (p′ Or p̂) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a correct description of p′?
A. A point estimate of p found by X divided by n.
B. The true population success probability.
C. The z-score for the sample.
D. The complement of q.
**Answer**: A

#### Complement Proportions (q And q′) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The complement of p′ is defined as ___.
**Answer**: 1 − p′

#### Complement Proportions (q And q′) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the error bound formula for a proportion, q′ represents:
A. The sample mean
B. 1 − p′
C. The population standard deviation
D. z_{α/2}
**Answer**: B

#### Normal Approximation For Sample Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For large n with p not near 0 or 1, P′ is approximately ___ with mean p and standard deviation sqrt(pq/n).
**Answer**: normal

#### Normal Approximation For Sample Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Under suitable conditions, the distribution of P′ is approximated by:
A. t with n − 1 df
B. Binomial with parameters n and p
C. Normal with mean p and standard deviation sqrt(pq/n)
D. Uniform on [0,1]
**Answer**: C

#### Z-Score For Sample Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standardized statistic for a sample proportion is z = ___.
**Answer**: (p′ − p) / sqrt(pq/n)

#### Z-Score For Sample Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula correctly standardizes a sample proportion under the normal model?
A. z = (p′ − p) / sqrt(pq/n)
B. z = (X − np) / sqrt(pq)
C. z = (p − p′) / sqrt(p′q′/n)
D. z = (p′ − q′)/n
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A two-sided confidence interval for p has the form (p′ − ___, p′ + ___).
**Answer**: EBP

#### Confidence Interval For A Population Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression gives a two-sided confidence interval for p at confidence level 1 − α?
A. p′ ± z_{α/2} sqrt(p′q′/n)
B. p ± z_{α/2} sqrt(pq/n)
C. p′ ± t_{α/2} s/√n
D. p′ ± z sqrt(pq)
**Answer**: A

#### Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The margin of error for a proportion is EBP = z_{α/2} × ___.
**Answer**: sqrt(p′ q′ / n)

#### Error Bound For A Proportion (EBP) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about the error bound EBP is correct?
A. It uses p′ and q′ because p and q are unknown.
B. It equals s/√n.
C. It is z times sqrt(pq) with no n.
D. It is computed only when σ is known.
**Answer**: A

#### Conditions For Proportion Confidence Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The method for confidence intervals in this section requires np′ > 5 and ___ > 5.
**Answer**: nq′

#### Conditions For Proportion Confidence Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which condition must hold to use the normal confidence interval for p?
A. Both np′ and nq′ exceed five.
B. n > 30 regardless of p′.
C. p′ > 0.5.
D. The sample is exactly 100.
**Answer**: A

#### Identifying A Proportion Problem (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A problem that counts successes out of n with no mention of a mean or average is a ___ problem.
**Answer**: proportion

#### Identifying A Proportion Problem (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an indicator that a problem involves a proportion?
A. The variable is quantitative with a known σ.
B. It involves counts of successes in n trials.
C. It asks for the sample mean.
D. It uses a t-interval.
**Answer**: B

#### “Plus-Four” Confidence Interval For p (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the plus-four method, the adjusted sample size is ___.
**Answer**: n + 4

#### “Plus-Four” Confidence Interval For p (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When should the plus-four method be used, according to the section?
A. Always, regardless of n or CL.
B. When CL ≥ 90% and sample size ≥ 10.
C. Only when np′ and nq′ < 5.
D. Only for small z values like 1.0.
**Answer**: B

#### Sample Size For Estimating A Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The required sample size to achieve margin EBP is n = ___.
**Answer**: (z_{α/2}^2 p′ q′) / EBP^2

#### Sample Size For Estimating A Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: To ensure a desired margin of error for estimating p, researchers should use:
A. n = (z_{α/2}^2 p′ q′) / EBP^2
B. n = z_{α/2} s/EBP
C. n = (t^2 σ^2)/EBP^2
D. Double the sample until np′ > 5
**Answer**: A

#### Conservative Choice Of p′ For Sample Size (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When p′ is unknown, a conservative choice is p′ = ___ to maximize p′q′.
**Answer**: 0.5

#### Conservative Choice Of p′ For Sample Size (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why is p′ = 0.5 used when planning n if p′ is unknown?
A. It minimizes the required sample size.
B. It maximizes p′q′ at 0.25, yielding the largest required n.
C. It matches the population mean.
D. It guarantees np′ > 5.
**Answer**: B

#### Confidence Level And α (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For confidence level CL, the significance level is α = ___.
**Answer**: 1 − CL

#### Confidence Level And α (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a 90% confidence interval for a proportion, z_{α/2} corresponds to:
A. A z-value with 5% in the right tail and 95% to the left.
B. A z-value with 10% in the right tail and 90% to the left.
C. A z-value with mean 0.90.
D. A z-value with 10% in each tail.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_107

#### Confidence Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A confidence interval for the mean home cost is written as ___.
**Answer**: x̄ ± EBM

#### Confidence Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes a 90% confidence interval in this study?
A. An interval that, in repeated samples, would contain the true mean about 90% of the time.
B. An interval that contains 90% of the sample home prices.
C. An interval that contains the true mean with probability exactly 0.90 for this one sample.
D. An interval guaranteed to include the true mean.
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Level (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The confidence level (for example, 90%) is the long-run success rate of the ___ capturing the true mean.
**Answer**: confidence intervals

#### Confidence Level (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In this activity, the confidence level refers to:
A. The percent of times the method would capture the true mean in repeated sampling.
B. The percent of data values that fall inside the computed interval.
C. The probability any individual home’s price falls in the interval.
D. The chance that the sample mean equals the population mean.
**Answer**: A

#### Alpha (α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a 90% confidence interval, the combined area in both tails is α = ___.
**Answer**: 0.10

#### Alpha (α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Alpha (α) represents:
A. The total area in both tails outside the confidence interval.
B. The area in one tail only.
C. The sample standard deviation.
D. The sample size.
**Answer**: A

#### Tail Area (α/2) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a 90% confidence interval, the area in each tail is α/2 = ___.
**Answer**: 0.05

#### Tail Area (α/2) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does α/2 represent when building a two-sided confidence interval?
A. The area in each tail outside the interval.
B. The total area under the curve.
C. Half of the sample size.
D. The margin of error.
**Answer**: A

#### Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The confidence interval endpoints are x̄ ± ___.
**Answer**: EBM

#### Error Bound For The Mean (EBM) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about the error bound for the mean (EBM) is correct?
A. It equals half the width of the confidence interval.
B. It is the same as the sample mean.
C. It increases when the sample standard deviation decreases, holding the confidence level fixed.
D. It does not depend on the sample size.
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Mean (X̄) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The symbol for the sample mean of the sale prices is ___.
**Answer**: x̄

#### Sample Mean (X̄) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In this study, X̄ represents:
A. The mean of the sample sale prices.
B. The population mean cost of all homes.
C. The median of the sample sale prices.
D. The sample standard deviation.
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Sx) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample standard deviation used in the interval is denoted by ___.
**Answer**: sx

#### Sample Standard Deviation (Sx) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The value sx is computed from:
A. The sample sale prices you recorded.
B. All home prices in the county.
C. The confidence interval limits.
D. A theoretical distribution only.
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Size (N) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In this activity, the sample size is n = ___.
**Answer**: 35

#### Sample Size (N) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If you increase the sample size while keeping variability and confidence level the same, the confidence interval will:
A. Become narrower.
B. Become wider.
C. Stay the same width.
D. Shift upward but keep the same width.
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable X̄ (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In words, X̄ is the random variable for the sample’s ___.
**Answer**: mean sale price

#### Random Variable X̄ (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the random variable X̄ in this activity?
A. The mean sale price from a random sample of homes.
B. The price of a single randomly chosen home.
C. The population mean price across all homes.
D. The number of homes in the sample.
**Answer**: A

#### Estimated Distribution For X̄ (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When the population standard deviation is unknown, the sampling distribution of X̄ is modeled by a ___ with df = n − 1.
**Answer**: t-distribution

#### Estimated Distribution For X̄ (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which distribution is appropriate for constructing the mean’s interval here (σ unknown, n = 35)?
A. t-distribution with df = n − 1.
B. Standard normal (z) regardless of n.
C. Uniform distribution on the sample range.
D. Chi-square distribution for variance.
**Answer**: A

#### Confidence Interval Limits (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: On the number line, the ends of the confidence interval are called the ___.
**Answer**: limits

#### Confidence Interval Limits (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How do you compute the confidence interval limits?
A. Lower = x̄ − EBM, Upper = x̄ + EBM.
B. Lower = x̄ − sx, Upper = x̄ + sx.
C. Lower = μ − EBM, Upper = μ + EBM.
D. Lower = x̄ − n, Upper = x̄ + n.
**Answer**: A

#### Interval Width (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The interval width equals 2 × ___.
**Answer**: EBM

#### Interval Width (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the EBM doubles while x̄ stays the same, the interval width will:
A. Double.
B. Be halved.
C. Stay the same.
D. Become zero.
**Answer**: A

#### Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: We are 90% confident the mean home cost lies ___ the two limits.
**Answer**: between

#### Interpretation Of A Confidence Interval (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is an appropriate interpretation of the 90% confidence interval for this study?
A. We are 90% confident the mean cost of a home in this area is between the calculated lower and upper limits.
B. Exactly 90% of the sampled home prices fall between the limits.
C. There is a 90% chance the true mean changed to fit inside this specific interval.
D. Any single future home price has a 90% chance to fall inside the interval.
**Answer**: A

#### Effect Of Confidence Level On EBM And Width (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: As the confidence level increases (same data), the EBM ___.
**Answer**: increases

#### Effect Of Confidence Level On EBM And Width (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When the confidence level rises from 80% to 99% using the same sample, the interval becomes:
A. Wider because EBM increases.
B. Narrower because EBM decreases.
C. Unchanged because EBM is fixed.
D. Shifted higher but the same width.
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: A

A. 0.10

D. 0.01
**Answer**: A

#### Tail Area (Alpha/2) (Q1)

**Answer**: 0.05

#### Tail Area (Alpha/2) (Q2)

A. 0.05

C. 0.90
D. 0.01
**Answer**: A

A. the sample proportion born in this state

D. the error bound

#### Sample Size (n) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In this survey, the sample size is denoted by ___.

#### Sample Size (n) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does n represent in this activity?
A. the number of students surveyed

B. n
C. x
D. α

#### Estimated Distribution (Q1)

## Section: seg_111

#### Confidence Interval (CI) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A confidence interval is an ___ for an unknown population parameter.
**Answer**: interval estimate

#### Confidence Interval (CI) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is the correct interpretation of a 90% confidence interval for a mean?
A. 90% of all data values lie in the interval.
B. 90% of future sample means will fall in the interval.
C. About 90% of intervals from repeated samples will contain the true mean.
D. The interval contains the true mean with probability 0.90 after seeing the data.
**Answer**: C

#### Confidence Level (CL) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The confidence level is the percent probability that the interval ___ the true population parameter.
**Answer**: contains the true population parameter

#### Confidence Level (CL) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If CL = 90%, about how many out of 100 such intervals will contain the true mean?
A. 85
B. 90
C. 95
D. 100
**Answer**: B

#### Confidence Interval General Form (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A confidence interval has the form (___ − EBM, ___ + EBM).
**Answer**: point estimate

#### Confidence Interval General Form (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In (x̄ − EBM, x̄ + EBM), what is the center of the interval?
A. x̄
B. μ
C. EBM
D. σ/√n
**Answer**: A

#### Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The error bound for a population mean (EBM) is also called the ___.
**Answer**: margin of error

#### Error Bound For A Population Mean (EBM) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which change will decrease the EBM, all else equal?
A. Increase the confidence level
B. Increase the population standard deviation
C. Increase the sample size
D. Decrease the sample size
**Answer**: C

#### Point Estimate (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a confidence interval for a population mean, the point estimate is the ___.
**Answer**: sample mean (x̄)

#### Point Estimate (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a point estimate for a population proportion?
A. p′
B. μ
C. σ
D. z(α/2)
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample standard deviation is denoted by ___.
**Answer**: s

#### Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In CI formulas, σ refers to the:
A. population standard deviation
B. sample standard deviation
C. standard error
D. point estimate
**Answer**: A

#### Normal Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The notation for a normal distribution is X ~ ___(μ, σ).
**Answer**: N

#### Normal Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is true of a normal distribution?
A. It is discrete with mean μ.
B. It is continuous with mean μ and standard deviation σ.
C. It is uniform on [0,1].
D. It must have μ = 0 and σ = 1.
**Answer**: B

#### Standard Normal Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A normal distribution with μ = 0 and σ = 1 is called the ___.
**Answer**: standard normal distribution

#### Standard Normal Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which symbol typically denotes a value from the standard normal distribution?
A. z
B. t
C. x̄
D. p′
**Answer**: A

#### Critical Z-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The critical z-value z(α/2) has area ___ to the right.
**Answer**: α/2

#### Critical Z-Value (Q2)

A. z(α/2)
B. t(α/2)
C. s
D. p′
**Answer**: A

#### Alpha (α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a confidence level CL, α = 1 − ___.
**Answer**: CL

#### Alpha (α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If CL = 95%, what is α?
A. 0.05
B. 0.95
C. 0.975
D. 0.025
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard deviation of the sampling distribution of x̄ is ___.
**Answer**: σ/√n

**Multiple Choice**: Under normality (or by the CLT), the sampling distribution of x̄ is:
A. Normal with mean μ and standard deviation σ/√n
B. t with df = n − 1 and mean μ
C. Binomial with parameters n and p
D. Uniform on (μ − σ, μ + σ)
**Answer**: A

A. Normal (z)
B. Student’s t
C. Chi-square
D. Uniform
**Answer**: A

#### Effect Of Confidence Level And Sample Size On Margin Of Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: As the confidence level increases, the EBM ___.
**Answer**: increases

**Multiple Choice**: Which change will narrow a confidence interval for μ (σ known)?
A. Increase the confidence level
B. Decrease the sample size
C. Increase the population standard deviation
D. Increase the sample size
**Answer**: D

## Section: seg_115

#### Null Hypothesis (H0) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis (H0) is a statement of ___ between sample means or proportions.
**Answer**: no difference

#### Null Hypothesis (H0) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the null hypothesis (H0)?
A. A claim about the population that we conclude when we reject H0.
B. A statement of no difference that includes an equality symbol.
C. A description of the sample data without symbols.
D. A rule that never uses equality symbols.
**Answer**: B

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis (Ha) never includes an ___ symbol.
**Answer**: equality

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For the wording "the mean GPA is different from 2.0," which Ha is appropriate?
A. μ = 2.0
B. μ ≠ 2.0
C. μ ≥ 2.0
D. μ ≤ 2.0
**Answer**: B

#### Decision Options In Hypothesis Testing (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When the evidence is insufficient, the decision is to ___ H0.
**Answer**: do not reject

#### Decision Options In Hypothesis Testing (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When should you reject H0?
A. When the sample information favors Ha.
B. When the sample information is insufficient.
C. Whenever H0 uses an equality symbol.
D. Never; you only decline to reject.
**Answer**: A

#### Symbol Conventions For Hypotheses (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: H0 uses symbols with ___, while Ha uses ≠, >, or <.
**Answer**: equality

#### Symbol Conventions For Hypotheses (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which pair follows the symbol conventions for H0 and Ha?
A. H0: μ = 66; Ha: μ ≠ 66
B. H0: μ ≠ 66; Ha: μ = 66
C. H0: μ > 66; Ha: μ ≥ 66
D. H0: μ < 66; Ha: μ ≤ 66
**Answer**: A

#### Acceptable Equality Notation In H0 (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Using '=' in H0 is acceptable even when Ha uses '>' or '<' because we only decide to ___ or not reject H0.
**Answer**: reject

#### Acceptable Equality Notation In H0 (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With Ha: μ > 5, which statement about H0 is acceptable?
A. H0 must be μ ≥ 5; using '=' is not allowed.
B. H0 may be written as μ = 5 because decisions are only reject or not reject H0.
C. H0 must be μ < 5 to avoid using equality.
D. H0 must be μ ≤ 5 and '=' is never acceptable.
**Answer**: B


---
## Section: seg_117

#### Type I Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Type I error occurs when you ___ a true null hypothesis.
**Answer**: reject

#### Type I Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which choice describes a Type I error?
A. Rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is true
C. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. Rejecting H0 when H0 is false
**Answer**: A

#### Type II Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Type II error occurs when you ___ the null hypothesis even though it is false.
**Answer**: do not reject

#### Type II Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a Type II error?
A. Rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
C. Rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is true
**Answer**: B

#### Alpha (α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The symbol α is the probability of a ___ error.
**Answer**: Type I error

#### Alpha (α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does α measure?
A. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Probability of not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
C. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. 1 − β
**Answer**: A

#### Beta (β) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: β is the probability of ___ the null hypothesis when it is false.
**Answer**: not rejecting

#### Beta (β) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement defines β?
A. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Probability of not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
C. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. 1 − β
**Answer**: B

#### Power Of The Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The Power of the Test is ___ .
**Answer**: 1 - β

#### Power Of The Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which describes the Power of the Test?
A. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is false
B. Probability of a Type I error
C. Probability of not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. Probability of rejecting H0 when H0 is true
**Answer**: A

D. p′

A. x̄
B. μ
C. p′
D. p

B. p′
C. μ
D. x̄

## Section: seg_121

#### Rare Event (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A "___" has occurred when a sample outcome would be very unlikely if the null hypothesis were true.
**Answer**: rare event

#### Rare Event (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the party example with 200 bubbles and only one $100 bill, Didi draws the $100 bill. Why does Ali doubt the "only one" claim?
A. Because such a rare event suggests the assumption may be wrong.
B. Because the sample mean increased to 17 cm.
C. Because α was set below 0.01.
D. Because the p-value must equal 0.50.
**Answer**: A

#### Null Hypothesis (H0) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis is the initial ___ about a population property that we test.
**Answer**: assumption

#### Null Hypothesis (H0) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which null hypothesis matches the claim "the mean bread height is more than 15 cm"?
A. H0: μ ≤ 15
B. H0: μ > 15
C. H0: μ = 17
D. H0: σ = 0.5
**Answer**: A

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The words "is more than" translate into the ___ hypothesis as a ">" statement.
**Answer**: alternative

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For the baker’s claim that the mean height is more than 15 cm, which is the correct Ha?
A. Ha: μ > 15
B. Ha: μ ≤ 15
C. Ha: μ = 15
D. Ha: μ ≥ 15
**Answer**: A

#### p-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The p-value is the probability that, if the ___ is true, results from another random sample would be as extreme or more extreme than those observed.
**Answer**: null hypothesis

#### p-Value (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does a very small p-value indicate?
A. Strong evidence against the null hypothesis.
B. Strong evidence for the null hypothesis.
C. A larger significance level was used.
D. The sample standard deviation is unknown.
**Answer**: A

#### Significance Level (Alpha, α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The significance level α is the preset probability of a ___ error.
**Answer**: Type I

#### Significance Level (Alpha, α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes α in a hypothesis test?
A. A chosen threshold to compare with the p-value.
B. The sample mean of the data.
C. The population standard deviation.
D. The computed p-value.
**Answer**: A

#### Type I Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Type I error occurs when we ___ the null hypothesis when it is true.
**Answer**: reject

#### Type I Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which action is a Type I error?
A. Rejecting H0 even though H0 is true.
B. Not rejecting H0 even though H0 is true.
C. Rejecting H0 because p-value ≥ α.
D. Not rejecting H0 even though H0 is false.
**Answer**: A

#### p-Value Decision Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Reject H0 when the p-value is ___ than the established alpha.
**Answer**: less

#### p-Value Decision Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If α = 0.05 and the p-value = 0.03, what is the decision?
A. Reject H0.
B. Do not reject H0.
C. Accept H0 as true.
D. Increase the sample size first.
**Answer**: A

#### Statistical Significance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample results are significant when the p-value is ___ α.
**Answer**: less than

#### Statistical Significance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When p-value ≥ α, the sample results are
A. Not significant.
B. Significant.
C. Always conclusive.
D. Biased.
**Answer**: A

#### Interpretation Of Non-Rejection (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Not rejecting H0 means there is ___ evidence to cast serious doubt on H0.
**Answer**: insufficient

#### Interpretation Of Non-Rejection (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If α ≤ p-value, which statement is appropriate?
A. Do not reject H0; there is not sufficient evidence to support Ha.
B. Reject H0; the results are significant.
C. Accept H0 as a fact.
D. Increase α until H0 is rejected.
**Answer**: A

#### Conclusion Statement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: After making your decision, write a conclusion about the hypotheses in terms of the ___.
**Answer**: given problem

#### Conclusion Statement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Given H0: p = 0.50, Ha: p > 0.50, α = 0.01, and p-value = 0.025 for the boy-birth procedure, what is a correct conclusion?
A. Do not reject H0; there is not sufficient evidence at the 1% level that the procedure increases the chance of a boy.
B. Reject H0; the procedure increases the chance of a boy at the 1% level.
C. Accept Ha as true because p-value < 0.05.
D. The procedure guarantees a boy because p-value > α.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_123

#### Level Of Significance (Alpha) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If no level of significance is given, a common standard to use is ___.
**Answer**: 0.05

#### Level Of Significance (Alpha) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When is the level of significance α chosen?
A. Before collecting the sample data
B. After seeing the p-value
C. After computing the test statistic
D. Only if H0 is rejected
**Answer**: A

#### P-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The p-value is the area in the left tail, the right tail, or ___ between the two tails.
**Answer**: split evenly

#### P-Value (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes a p-value?
A. The probability H0 is true
B. The area in the relevant tail(s) for the observed sample, assuming H0 is true
C. The sample mean divided by σ
D. The preset level α
**Answer**: B

#### Left-, Right-, And Two-Tailed Tests (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If Ha: μ ≠ μ0, the test is ___.
**Answer**: two-tailed

#### Left-, Right-, And Two-Tailed Tests (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which alternative hypothesis leads to a right-tailed test?
A. Ha: p < 0.2
B. Ha: μ > 1
C. Ha: μ = 10
D. Ha: p ≠ 0.5
**Answer**: B

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Ha never has a symbol that contains an ___.
**Answer**: equal sign

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For H0: μ = 10, which Ha makes the test left-tailed?
A. Ha: μ < 10
B. Ha: μ > 10
C. Ha: μ = 10
D. Ha: μ ≤ 10
**Answer**: A

#### Decision Rule Using P-Values (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Reject H0 when the p-value is ___ than α.
**Answer**: less

#### Decision Rule Using P-Values (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With α = 0.05 and p-value = 0.0187, what is the decision?
A. Do not reject H0 because p-value > α
B. Reject H0 because p-value < α
C. Do not reject H0 because α < p-value
D. Reject H0 because α is preset
**Answer**: B

#### Evidence Strength From P-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A smaller p-value gives ___ confidence in rejecting H0.
**Answer**: more

#### Evidence Strength From P-Value (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using α = 0.05, which p-value gives stronger evidence against H0?
A. 0.04
B. 0.001
C. 0.056
D. 0.40
**Answer**: B

#### Null Hypothesis Parameter Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In setting up the test distribution for a mean, the value of μ comes from ___, not the data.
**Answer**: H0

#### Null Hypothesis Parameter Value (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Example 9.14, which μ is used in X̄ ~ N(μ, σ/√n)?
A. 16 (the sample mean)
B. 16.43 from H0
C. 0.8 (σ)
D. 15 (n)
**Answer**: B

#### Type I Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Type I error is to ___ H0 when H0 is ___.
**Answer**: reject; true

#### Type I Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which describes a Type I error?
A. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Rejecting H0 when H0 is true
C. Rejecting H0 when H0 is false
D. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
**Answer**: B

#### Type II Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A Type II error is to ___ H0 when H0 is ___.
**Answer**: not reject; false

#### Type II Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which describes a Type II error?
A. Rejecting H0 when H0 is true
B. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is false
C. Not rejecting H0 when H0 is true
D. Rejecting H0 when H0 is false
**Answer**: B

#### Z-Test For A Single Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When σ is known for a population mean, use a ___-test.
**Answer**: Z-test

#### Z-Test For A Single Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which test is appropriate when testing a mean with σ known and normal data?
A. t-test for a single mean
B. Z-test for a single mean
C. One-proportion z-test
D. Chi-square test
**Answer**: B

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean (Known σ) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When σ is known, the standard deviation of X̄ is ___.
**Answer**: σ/√n

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Mean (Known σ) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When σ is known, X̄ has which distribution?
A. Normal with mean μ and SD σ/√n
B. Normal with mean x̄ and SD s/√n
C. t with df = n − 1
D. Uniform with mean μ
**Answer**: A

#### T-Test For A Single Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: With σ unknown and normal data, a test of a single mean uses the ___ distribution.
**Answer**: Student's t

#### T-Test For A Single Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A sample of n = 10 from a normal population with unknown σ should be tested using which?
A. Z-test for a mean
B. One-proportion z-test
C. t-test for a single mean
D. Two-sample z-test
**Answer**: C

#### Degrees Of Freedom (t-Test) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a single-mean t-test with n = 10, df = ___.
**Answer**: 9

#### Degrees Of Freedom (t-Test) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the formula for degrees of freedom in a single-mean t-test?
A. df = n
B. df = n − 1
C. df = n + 1
D. df = n − 2
**Answer**: B

#### One-Proportion Z-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A hypothesis test for a single population proportion uses a ___.
**Answer**: one-proportion z-test

#### One-Proportion Z-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which scenario uses a one-proportion z-test?
A. Testing μ with σ known
B. Testing μ with σ unknown
C. Testing the proportion p with sample counts x and n
D. Comparing two means
**Answer**: C

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For sample proportion P′, the variance is ___.
**Answer**: pq/n

#### Sampling Distribution Of The Sample Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The sampling distribution of P′ has mean ___ and variance ___.
A. p; pq/n
B. p′; pq/n
C. μ; σ/√n
D. 0; 1
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Proportion (P′) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample proportion is computed as p′ = ___.
**Answer**: x/n

#### Sample Proportion (P′) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If x = 53 and n = 100, then p′ equals which value?
A. 0.47
B. 0.53
C. 0.50
D. 53
**Answer**: B

#### Conditions For One-Proportion Z-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A condition for using the one-proportion z-test is that both np and nq are ___.
**Answer**: sufficiently large

#### Conditions For One-Proportion Z-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which set lists the conditions for a one-proportion z-test?
A. Normal data and unknown σ
B. Known σ and large n
C. Sufficiently large np and nq, two independent outcomes, and fixed p
D. Random assignment and matched pairs
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_125

#### Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In hypothesis testing, a ___ is a statement about a population parameter.

#### Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes a hypothesis in this chapter?
A. A statement about a population parameter.
B. A calculation of a sample statistic.
C. A graph of the sampling distribution.
D. A rule to compare α and p-value.
**Answer**: A

#### Null Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis, denoted ___, must include an equality (=, ≤, or ≥).
**Answer**: H0

#### Null Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which form is a valid null hypothesis?
A. μ < 10
B. μ ≠ 10
C. μ ≥ 10
D. p > 0.5
**Answer**: C

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis is written using ___.
**Answer**: <, >, or ≠

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a valid alternative hypothesis?
A. μ = 20
B. μ ≤ 20
C. p ≥ 0.4
D. p ≠ 0.4
**Answer**: D

#### Hypothesis Testing (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Hypothesis testing uses sample evidence to decide whether to ___ the stated hypothesis.
**Answer**: reject or not reject H0

#### Hypothesis Testing (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes hypothesis testing?
A. A procedure using sample evidence to decide about a claim.
B. A method to compute confidence intervals only.
C. A way to prove a claim true.
D. A graphing technique for normal curves.
**Answer**: A

#### Level Of Significance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The level of significance (___) is the probability of a Type I error.
**Answer**: α

#### Level Of Significance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about the level of significance?
A. It equals the p-value.
B. It is preset before the test.
C. It is the probability of a Type II error.
D. It must be 0.10.
**Answer**: B

#### p-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The ___ is the probability of the observed result (or more extreme) assuming H0 is true.
**Answer**: p-value

#### p-Value (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A smaller p-value indicates:
A. Stronger evidence against H0.
B. Less probability of a rare event.
C. A lower preset α.
D. That H0 is proven true.
**Answer**: A

#### Type I Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ occurs when a true null hypothesis is rejected.
**Answer**: Type I error

#### Type I Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which describes a Type I error?
A. Rejecting a true H0.
B. Not rejecting a true H0.
C. Rejecting a false Ha.
D. Not rejecting a false H0.
**Answer**: A

#### Type II Error (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ occurs when a false null hypothesis is not rejected.
**Answer**: Type II error

#### Type II Error (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which describes a Type II error?
A. Rejecting a true H0.
B. Not rejecting a false H0.
C. Rejecting a false H0.
D. Not rejecting a true H0.
**Answer**: B

#### Power Of The Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The power of a test is ___.

**Answer**: B

**Fill-in-the-blank**: By the Central Limit Theorem, for sufficiently large n, the distribution of X̄ is approximately ___.
**Answer**: normal

#### Central Limit Theorem (Q2)

B. Population becomes normal as n increases.
C. Sample sums have variance zero.
D. Sample proportions are always t-distributed.
**Answer**: A

A. σ
B. σ/√n
C. s
D. σ^2/√n
**Answer**: B

#### Decision Rule Using p-Value (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If α > p-value, you ___ H0.

A. Reject H0.
B. Not reject H0.

**Answer**: B

**Fill-in-the-blank**: Use a Student's t-test for a single mean when the population standard deviation is ___.
**Answer**: unknown

#### Student's t-Test For A Single Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which test is appropriate: simple random sample, n = 20, population approximately normal, σ unknown?
A. Normal test for a single mean
B. Student's t-test for a single mean
C. Normal test for a single proportion
D. Chi-square test
**Answer**: B

#### Normal Test For A Single Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Use the normal test for a single mean when the population standard deviation is ___.
**Answer**: known

#### Normal Test For A Single Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which distribution do you use when testing a single mean with known σ and n ≥ 30?
A. t-distribution
B. Normal distribution
C. Binomial distribution
D. Uniform distribution
**Answer**: B

#### Normal Test For A Single Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a single proportion, the normal test requires both np and nq > ___.
**Answer**: 5

#### Normal Test For A Single Proportion (Q2)

A. Binomial
B. Normal
C. t
D. Uniform
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)

## Section: seg_129

#### Aspin-Welch T-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test comparing two independent population means when the population standard deviations are unknown and may be unequal is called the ___.
**Answer**: Aspin-Welch t-test

#### Aspin-Welch T-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation calls for the Aspin-Welch t-test?
A. Comparing two independent means with unknown and possibly unequal population standard deviations.
B. Comparing one sample mean to a known population mean with a known population standard deviation.
C. Comparing two proportions from independent samples.
D. Comparing paired measurements from the same individuals.
**Answer**: A

#### Assumptions For Independent Two-Sample Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A key assumption is that the two samples are ___ random samples from distinct populations.
**Answer**: simple

#### Assumptions For Independent Two-Sample Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about distributional assumptions is correct?
A. For small samples, the population distributions should be normal; for large samples, normality is not required.
B. For large samples, the population distributions must be normal; for small samples, they are not important.
C. Normality is required regardless of sample size.
D. No assumptions about independence are needed.
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Error Of The Difference In Means (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard error for X̄1 − X̄2 uses (s1^2)/n1 plus ___.
**Answer**: (s2^2)/n2

#### Standard Error Of The Difference In Means (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression represents the standard error of X̄1 − X̄2?
A. sqrt((s1^2)/n1 + (s2^2)/n2)
B. (s1 + s2)/(n1 + n2)
C. sqrt(s1^2 + s2^2)
D. (x̄1 − x̄2)/(s1 s2)
**Answer**: A

#### Two-Sample t Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standardized difference in sample means is a ___-score.
**Answer**: t

#### Two-Sample t Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression best describes the two-sample t test statistic?
A. ((x̄1 − x̄2) − (μ1 − μ2)) divided by the standard error.
B. (x̄1 − x̄2) divided by the pooled variance.
C. (μ1 − μ2) divided by the standard error.
D. (x̄1 − μ1) divided by s1/√n1.
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Welch’s Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For Welch’s test, the degrees of freedom are not always a ___ number.
**Answer**: whole

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Welch’s Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about the degrees of freedom used with the two-sample Welch–Aspin t-test?
A. They may be non-integer and are typically computed by a calculator or computer.
B. They always equal n1 + n2 − 2.
C. They equal the smaller of n1 − 1 and n2 − 1.
D. They are not needed because the test uses the normal distribution.
**Answer**: A

#### Non-Pooled Variances (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In this procedure, the sample variances are ___ pooled.
**Answer**: not

#### Non-Pooled Variances (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When comparing two means with unknown and possibly unequal population standard deviations, how should variances be handled?
A. Do not pool the sample variances.
B. Always pool the sample variances.
C. Replace both sample variances with a common population variance.
D. Ignore the variances if samples are small.
**Answer**: A

#### Distributional Approximation Conditions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When n1 + n2 > 30, you can use the ___ distribution to approximate Student’s t.
**Answer**: normal

#### Distributional Approximation Conditions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When is the Student’s t approximation considered very good for the Welch test?
A. When both n1 and n2 are at least five.
B. When n1 + n2 equals five.
C. Only when both n1 and n2 are at least 30.
D. Only when the populations are non-normal.
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable For Two-Sample Comparison (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The random variable for the two-sample comparison is ___, the difference between sample means.
**Answer**: X̄1 − X̄2

#### Random Variable For Two-Sample Comparison (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which of the following represents the random variable in a two-sample mean comparison?
A. X̄1 − X̄2
B. μ1 − μ2
C. s1 − s2
D. X1 − X2
**Answer**: A

#### Cohen’s d (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Cohen’s d equals the difference between two means divided by the ___.
**Answer**: pooled standard deviation

#### Cohen’s d (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula matches Cohen’s d?
A. (x̄1 − x̄2)/s_pooled
B. (x̄1 − x̄2)/standard error
C. (μ1 − μ2)/s_pooled
D. (x̄1 + x̄2)/s_pooled
**Answer**: A

#### Cohen’s Standard Effect Sizes (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: According to Cohen’s standards, d = 0.2 is considered a ___ effect.
**Answer**: small

#### Cohen’s Standard Effect Sizes (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which value corresponds to a medium effect size under Cohen’s standards?
A. 0.1
B. 0.5
C. 0.8
D. 1.2
**Answer**: B

#### Pooled Standard Deviation (For Cohen’s d) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The pooled standard deviation combines variability from ___ samples.
**Answer**: both

#### Pooled Standard Deviation (For Cohen’s d) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which inputs are needed to compute the pooled standard deviation used in Cohen’s d?
A. The two sample standard deviations and their sample sizes.
B. Only the two sample means.
C. The population standard deviations.
D. Only one sample’s standard deviation and size.
**Answer**: A
