## Section: seg_131

#### Independent Means With Known Population Standard Deviations (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When comparing two population means with known σ1 and σ2 and normal populations, the appropriate test is a ___.
**Answer**: two-sample z-test

#### Independent Means With Known Population Standard Deviations (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the floor wax example with σ1 = 0.33 and σ2 = 0.36 known and normal populations, which procedure is appropriate?
A. Two-sample z-test for means
B. Paired t-test
C. One-sample z-test
D. Chi-square test for variances
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable X̄1 − X̄2 (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The random variable used to compare the two means is ___.
**Answer**: X̄1 − X̄2

#### Random Variable X̄1 − X̄2 (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which random variable is modeled in these two-sample mean tests?
A. X1 − X2
B. X̄1 − X̄2
C. μ1 − μ2
D. s1 − s2
**Answer**: B

#### Sampling Distribution Of X̄1 − X̄2 (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: With known σ1 and σ2 and normal populations, X̄1 − X̄2 ~ ___.
**Answer**: N(μ1 − μ2, (σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2)

#### Sampling Distribution Of X̄1 − X̄2 (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the sampling distribution of X̄1 − X̄2 under the known-σ, normal-populations conditions?
A. Normal with mean μ1 − μ2 and parameter (σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2
B. Student’s t with df = n1 + n2 − 2
C. Chi-square with df = n1 + n2 − 2
D. Exponential with rate μ1 − μ2
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Deviation Of X̄1 − X̄2 (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard deviation used in the z calculation for X̄1 − X̄2 is ___.
**Answer**: (σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2

#### Standard Deviation Of X̄1 − X̄2 (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression is used for the standard deviation of X̄1 − X̄2 in this section?
A. (σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2
B. σ1/√n1 − σ2/√n2
C. √[(σ1)² + (σ2)²]
D. (σ1 + σ2)/(n1 + n2)
**Answer**: A

#### Z-Test Statistic For Two Population Means (Known σ1, σ2) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For known σ1 and σ2, the test statistic is z = ___.
**Answer**: [(x̄1 − x̄2) − (μ1 − μ2)] ÷ [(σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2]

#### Z-Test Statistic For Two Population Means (Known σ1, σ2) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula matches the z statistic used in Example 10.6?
A. [(x̄1 − x̄2) − (μ1 − μ2)] ÷ [(σ1)²/n1 + (σ2)²/n2]
B. (x̄1 − x̄2) ÷ √[(s1²/n1) + (s2²/n2)]
C. [(μ1 − μ2) − (x̄1 − x̄2)] ÷ s/√n
D. (x̄1 − x̄2) ÷ (σ/√n)
**Answer**: A

#### Hypotheses For Comparing Two Means (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To test whether wax 1 lasts longer than wax 2, state the alternative hypothesis: ___.
**Answer**: Ha: μ1 > μ2

#### Hypotheses For Comparing Two Means (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which null hypothesis pairs with Ha: μ1 > μ2 for a right-tailed comparison of two means?
A. H0: μ1 = μ2
B. H0: μ1 ≥ μ2
C. H0: μ1 ≤ μ2
D. H0: μ1 ≠ μ2
**Answer**: C

#### Right-Tailed Test Determination (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The phrase 'is more effective (lasts longer)' leads to a ___-tailed test.
**Answer**: right

#### Right-Tailed Test Determination (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which wording suggests using a right-tailed test?
A. is more effective
B. is less than
C. is different from
D. is not equal to
**Answer**: A

#### p-Value Decision Rule (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If α = 0.05 and the p-value = 0.1799, you ___ H0.
**Answer**: do not reject H0

#### p-Value Decision Rule (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: According to the rule in this section, when α < p-value, you should:
A. reject H0
B. do not reject H0
C. accept Ha
D. increase α until p-value < α
**Answer**: B

#### Assumptions For Validity (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For the two-sample z test with known σ1 and σ2, both populations must be ___.
**Answer**: normal

#### Assumptions For Validity (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which set of conditions is required for the two-sample z test used here?
A. Two independent groups; both populations normal
B. Paired data; both samples large enough
C. Two independent groups; both populations exponential
D. Dependent samples; both populations normal
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_133

#### Conditions For Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The two samples must be independent ___ to use a two-proportion test.
**Answer**: simple random samples

#### Conditions For Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is NOT one of the stated conditions for comparing two independent population proportions?
A. Independent simple random samples
B. At least five successes and five failures in each sample
C. Each population at least 10 or 20 times the sample size
D. Samples are matched pairs
**Answer**: D

#### Hypothesis Test For Two Independent Population Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A two-proportion hypothesis test helps decide if a difference in sample proportions reflects a difference in the ___ proportions.
**Answer**: population

#### Hypothesis Test For Two Independent Population Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the goal of a hypothesis test for two independent population proportions?
A. To compare two sample means
B. To decide if a difference in sample proportions reflects a population difference
C. To estimate a single population proportion
D. To analyze paired data
**Answer**: B

#### Null Hypothesis For Two Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The usual null hypothesis for two proportions states ___
**Answer**: pA = pB

#### Null Hypothesis For Two Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement represents the null hypothesis for two proportions?
A. pA − pB = 0
B. pA − pB ≠ 0
C. pA − pB > 0
D. pA − pB < 0
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable For Two-Proportion Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The random variable is the difference in sample proportions, written as ___.
**Answer**: P′A − P′B

#### Random Variable For Two-Proportion Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression is the random variable in a two-proportion z test?
A. p′A
B. p′B
C. p′A − p′B
D. pA − pB
**Answer**: C

#### Pooled Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Under H0, the pooled proportion is computed as ___.
**Answer**: (xA + xB) / (nA + nB)

#### Pooled Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Given xA = 20, nA = 200, xB = 12, and nB = 200, what is the pooled proportion pc?
A. 0.08
B. 0.10
C. 0.06
D. 0.04
**Answer**: A

#### Sampling Distribution Of Difference In Sample Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Under the null hypothesis, P′A − P′B is approximately ___ with mean 0.
**Answer**: normal

#### Sampling Distribution Of Difference In Sample Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What variance is used for the sampling distribution of P′A − P′B under H0?
A. pc(1 − pc)(1/nA + 1/nB)
B. pA(1 − pA)/nA + pB(1 − pB)/nB
C. s1^2/n1 + s2^2/n2
D. pc(1 − pc)/(nA + nB)
**Answer**: A

#### Two-Proportion Z-Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The z statistic is [(p′A − p′B) − (pA − pB)] divided by ___.
**Answer**: sqrt[pc(1 − pc)(1/nA + 1/nB)]

#### Two-Proportion Z-Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression is the numerator of the two-proportion z statistic?
A. (p′A − p′B) − (pA − pB)
B. p′A − p′B
C. pA − pB
D. (p′A − p′B) − pc
**Answer**: A

#### Tail Selection For Two-Proportion Tests (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The phrase "is a difference" in the alternative hypothesis indicates a ___ test.
**Answer**: two-tailed

#### Tail Selection For Two-Proportion Tests (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which alternative hypothesis matches the wording "less than"?
A. pA − pB > 0
B. pA − pB < 0
C. pA − pB ≠ 0
D. pA = pB
**Answer**: B

#### Decision Rule Using P-Value And Alpha (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If α < p-value, you ___ reject H0.
**Answer**: do not

#### Decision Rule Using P-Value And Alpha (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Example 10.8 with α = 0.01 and p-value = 0.1404, what is the decision?
A. Reject H0
B. Do not reject H0
C. Accept Ha
D. Increase α until p-value < α
**Answer**: B

#### Sample Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample proportion is computed as p′ = ___.
**Answer**: x / n

#### Sample Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Example 10.8, what is the estimated proportion for group A with xA = 20 and nA = 200?
A. 0.10
B. 0.08
C. 0.06
D. 0.04
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_135

#### Matched Or Paired Samples (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a matched design, subjects are ___ in pairs and two measurements are taken from the same pair.
**Answer**: matched

#### Matched Or Paired Samples (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which design compares two measurements from the same pair by analyzing their differences?
A. Independent two-sample design
B. Matched or paired samples design
C. Single-proportion test
D. One-way ANOVA
**Answer**: B

#### Differences As Data (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a paired test, the ___ calculated from the matched measurements are the data used in the hypothesis test.
**Answer**: differences

#### Differences As Data (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What forms the sample used in a hypothesis test for matched pairs?
A. The before measurements only
B. The after measurements only
C. The paired differences
D. The combined totals
**Answer**: C

#### Population Mean Of Differences (μd) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The population mean of the paired differences is denoted ___.
**Answer**: μd

#### Population Mean Of Differences (μd) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In matched-pairs testing, μd represents:
A. Mean of the before measurements
B. Mean of the after measurements
C. Mean of the differences
D. Median of the differences
**Answer**: C

#### Paired t-Test For Mean Difference (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Testing μd in matched pairs uses a one-sample ___ on the differences.
**Answer**: Student's t test

#### Paired t-Test For Mean Difference (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which distribution is used for the matched-pairs test of μd?
A. Student's t with n − 1 degrees of freedom
B. Standard normal (z) regardless of n
C. Chi-square with n degrees of freedom
D. F distribution
**Answer**: A

#### Test Statistic For Paired t-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test statistic for the paired t-test is t = (___ − μd) / (sd/√n).
**Answer**: x̄d

#### Test Statistic For Paired t-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula correctly computes the t statistic for testing μd?
A. t = (x̄d − μd) / (sd/√n)
B. t = (μd − x̄d) / sd·n
C. t = x̄d / sd
D. t = (x̄d − μd) / sd
**Answer**: A

#### Assumptions For Paired Samples Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: One condition for using the paired t-test is that the paired differences come from a ___ population (or n is large).
**Answer**: normal

#### Assumptions For Paired Samples Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a required characteristic of a matched-pairs t-test?
A. Simple random sampling is used
B. The two groups are independent
C. Sample sizes must exceed 30
D. Population variances are known
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable X̄d (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Let ___ be the sample mean of the paired differences used in the test.
**Answer**: X̄d

#### Random Variable X̄d (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does X̄d represent in the matched-pairs test?
A. The population mean of differences
B. The sample mean of the differences
C. A single observed difference
D. The sample standard deviation of differences
**Answer**: B

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Paired t-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a paired t-test with n differences, the degrees of freedom are ___.
**Answer**: n − 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Paired t-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Example 10.11, there are n = 8 differences. What are the degrees of freedom for the test?
A. 7
B. 8
C. 15
D. 16
**Answer**: A


---
## Section: seg_137

#### Two Population Means With Unknown Standard Deviations (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For two independent means with unknown population standard deviations, the test uses the ___.
**Answer**: Student's t-distribution

#### Two Population Means With Unknown Standard Deviations (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In this test of two independent means with unknown standard deviations, the random variable is:
A. X̄1 − X̄2
B. p̂A − p̂B
C. x̄d
D. σ1 − σ2
**Answer**: A

#### Two Population Means With Known Standard Deviations (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When σ1 and σ2 are known, the sampling distribution of X̄1 − X̄2 is ___.
**Answer**: normal distribution

#### Two Population Means With Known Standard Deviations (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With known standard deviations for two independent means, the test statistic is a:
A. z-score
B. t-score
C. chi-square statistic
D. F-statistic
**Answer**: A

#### Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For two independent proportions, the random variable is ___.
**Answer**: p̂A − p̂B

#### Comparing Two Independent Population Proportions (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which distribution is used for the difference in estimated proportions?
A. Student's t-distribution
B. Normal distribution
C. Exponential distribution
D. Uniform distribution
**Answer**: B

#### Matched Or Paired Samples (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In matched samples, we test the mean of the differences d, denoted ___.
**Answer**: x̄d

#### Matched Or Paired Samples (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a paired t-test with n pairs, the degrees of freedom equal:
A. n
B. n + 1
C. n − 1
D. 2n − 2
**Answer**: C

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Df) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Degrees of freedom is the number of objects in a sample that are ___.
**Answer**: free to vary

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Df) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a paired t-test with 12 differences, the df is:
A. 12
B. 11
C. 10
D. 24
**Answer**: B

#### Pooled Proportion (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The pooled proportion estimates the common value of ___ in a two-proportion test.
**Answer**: p1 and p2

#### Pooled Proportion (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: According to the section, the pooled proportion pc is calculated as:
A. (xF + xM) / (nF + nM)
B. (p̂F + p̂M) / 2
C. (xF/nF) − (xM/nM)
D. (xF − xM) / (nF + nM)
**Answer**: A

#### Standard Deviation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The symbol for a population standard deviation is ___.
**Answer**: σ

#### Standard Deviation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes standard deviation?
A. It is the average of the data values.
B. It is the square of the variance.
C. It is the square root of the variance and measures spread around the mean.
D. It is the difference between the largest and smallest values.
**Answer**: C

#### Variable (Random Variable) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Random variables are typically denoted by ___ letters like X, Y, Z.
**Answer**: upper-case Latin

#### Variable (Random Variable) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a possible domain for X = hair color?
A. {0, 1, 2, 3}
B. {black, blond, gray, green, orange}
C. all real numbers
D. {short, medium, tall}
**Answer**: B

## Section: seg_141

#### Chi-Square Distribution Notation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The random variable is shown as ___, but may be any uppercase letter.
**Answer**: χ2

#### Chi-Square Distribution Notation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which notation states that a variable X follows a chi-square distribution with df degrees of freedom?
A. X ~ χ2 (with df)
B. X ~ N(μ, σ)
C. X ~ t(df)
D. X ~ F(df1, df2)
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For practice problems, the degrees of freedom can be taken as df = ___.
**Answer**: n - 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about degrees of freedom (df) for chi-square is true?
A. df depends on how chi-square is used; the three major uses calculate df differently.
B. df is always equal to n.
C. df is always 1.
D. df must be 0 for all chi-square tests.
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Mean (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a χ2 distribution, the population mean μ equals ___.
**Answer**: df

#### Chi-Square Mean (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If X ~ χ2 with 10 degrees of freedom, what is the population mean μ?
A. 10
B. 0
C. √20
D. 1
**Answer**: A

#### Sum Of Squared Standard Normals (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A χ2 random variable with k degrees of freedom is the sum of k independent, squared ___ variables.
**Answer**: standard normal

#### Sum Of Squared Standard Normals (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression represents a χ2 variable with k degrees of freedom?
A. Sum of k independent squared standard normals
B. Sum of k standard normals
C. Square of a single normal variable with any mean and variance
D. Sum of k independent t variables
**Answer**: A

#### Distribution Shape (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square curve is nonsymmetrical and skewed to the ___.
**Answer**: right

#### Distribution Shape (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the shape of the chi-square distribution?
A. Nonsymmetrical and skewed right
B. Symmetric and bell-shaped
C. Uniform on an interval
D. Bimodal
**Answer**: A

#### Family Of Chi-Square Curves (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: There is a different chi-square curve for each value of ___.
**Answer**: df

#### Family Of Chi-Square Curves (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: As df changes, what happens to the chi-square curve?
A. A different chi-square curve corresponds to each df.
B. It remains the same for all df.
C. It becomes a t-distribution curve.
D. It becomes uniform.
**Answer**: A

#### Nonnegativity Of Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test statistic is always greater than or equal to ___.
**Answer**: zero

#### Nonnegativity Of Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about the chi-square test statistic is true?
A. It is always ≥ 0.
B. It can be any real number.
C. It is always ≤ 0.
D. It must equal 1.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_143

#### Goodness-Of-Fit Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ determines whether sample data fit a specified distribution using a chi-square framework.
**Answer**: goodness-of-fit test

#### Goodness-Of-Fit Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which test should you use to check if observed counts across categories match a claimed distribution?
A. Goodness-of-fit test
B. Two-sample t-test
C. Linear regression
D. One-way ANOVA
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test statistic for a goodness-of-fit test is the sum of ___ across categories.
**Answer**: (O − E)^2 / E

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a goodness-of-fit test, which quantity grows larger when observed and expected counts differ more?
A. The sum of (O − E)^2 / E
B. The sum of observed counts
C. The mean of observed counts
D. The standard deviation of expected counts
**Answer**: A

#### Observed Values (O) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a goodness-of-fit test, the ___ are the data values recorded for each category.
**Answer**: observed values (O)

#### Observed Values (O) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the weekday absence example with counts 15, 12, 9, 9, 15, these numbers are the:
A. Observed values (O)
B. Expected values (E)
C. Residuals (O − E)
D. Category probabilities
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Values (E) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If the null hypothesis is true, the frequencies you would expect in each category are called ___.
**Answer**: expected values (E)

#### Expected Values (E) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When testing equal frequencies across five weekdays with 60 total absences, what is the expected count for each day under H0?
A. 12
B. 15
C. 60
D. 5
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a goodness-of-fit test with k categories, the degrees of freedom equal ___.
**Answer**: k − 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: After combining absenteeism categories to have four cells, what is the degrees of freedom?
A. 2
B. 3
C. 4
D. 5
**Answer**: B

#### Right-Tailed Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A goodness-of-fit test is almost always ___-tailed because large discrepancies push the statistic into the right tail.
**Answer**: right

#### Right-Tailed Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which tail is used to compute the p-value in a chi-square goodness-of-fit test?
A. Right tail only
B. Left tail only
C. Both tails
D. Middle 95%
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Count Requirement (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To validly use the chi-square goodness-of-fit test, each expected cell frequency should be at least ___.
**Answer**: five

#### Expected Count Requirement (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the absenteeism example, why were the '9–11' and '12+' groups combined?
A. The expected count in '12+' was less than five
B. To increase the total sample size
C. To reduce variance between cells
D. Because the observed count was zero
**Answer**: A

#### Hypotheses For Goodness-Of-Fit (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a goodness-of-fit test, H0 states that the data ___ the specified distribution, and Ha states they do not.
**Answer**: fit

#### Hypotheses For Goodness-Of-Fit (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which hypotheses are correct for testing whether absences are equally likely across weekdays?
A. H0: fit a uniform distribution; Ha: do not fit a uniform distribution
B. H0: mean = 0; Ha: mean ≠ 0
C. H0: proportions are increasing; Ha: proportions are decreasing
D. H0: variances are equal; Ha: variances differ
**Answer**: A

#### Test Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test statistic in a goodness-of-fit test is compared to a ___ distribution with df = k − 1.
**Answer**: chi-square

#### Test Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For five categories, which distribution and degrees of freedom are used?
A. Chi-square with 4 df
B. t with 4 df
C. Normal with mean 0 and sd 1
D. F with (4, n − 1) df
**Answer**: A

#### P-Value For Goodness-Of-Fit (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The p-value for a goodness-of-fit test is P(χ² ___ the calculated statistic) under the chi-square distribution with the appropriate degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: greater than

#### P-Value For Goodness-Of-Fit (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If χ² = 3 with df = 4, which calculator input gives the p-value as shown in the text?
A. χ2cdf(3, 1E99, 4)
B. χ2cdf(0, 3, 4)
C. normalcdf(3, 1E99, 4)
D. tcdf(3, 1E99, 4)
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_145

#### Test Of Independence (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A hypothesis test that uses a contingency table to decide if two factors are independent is called a ___.
**Answer**: test of independence

#### Test Of Independence (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation calls for a chi-square test of independence?
A. You have counts in a contingency table for two factors and want to know if they are independent.
B. You have counts for one factor and want to compare them to a claimed one-factor distribution.
C. You only want to compute an expected count for one cell without testing independence.
D. You only want to check if P(A AND B) equals P(A)+P(B).
**Answer**: A

#### Contingency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A table of observed data values organized by the categories of two factors is a ___.
**Answer**: contingency table

#### Contingency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In this section, the table of volunteer type by hours (Table 11.15) contains what kind of values?
A. Observed frequencies (data)
B. Expected frequencies
C. Probabilities for each cell
D. Row proportions
**Answer**: A

#### Observed Frequency (O) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the chi-square test, the data count recorded in a cell is the ___.
**Answer**: observed frequency (O)

#### Observed Frequency (O) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which symbol denotes the observed value in a contingency table cell?
A. O
B. E
C. i
D. j
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Frequency (E) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Under independence, the expected count for a cell is computed as ___.
**Answer**: (row total × column total) ÷ total surveyed

#### Expected Frequency (E) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What minimum expected count per cell is required to use this test?
A. 5
B. 1
C. 10
D. 30
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test statistic for a test of independence is the sum over all cells of ___.
**Answer**: (O − E)²/E

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How many terms are summed in the chi-square statistic for a table with i rows and j columns?
A. i·j
B. i + j
C. (i − 1)(j − 1)
D. i² + j²
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Independence Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The degrees of freedom for a test of independence is ___.
**Answer**: (number of columns − 1)(number of rows − 1)

#### Degrees Of Freedom For Independence Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a 3×3 table (3 columns, 3 rows), what is df?
A. 4
B. 6
C. 8
D. 9
**Answer**: A

#### Hypotheses For Independence Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a test of independence, the null hypothesis states the two factors are ___.
**Answer**: independent

#### Hypotheses For Independence Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which set states the correct null and alternative hypotheses for a test of independence?
A. H0: The factors are independent; Ha: The factors are dependent.
B. H0: The factors are dependent; Ha: The factors are independent.
C. H0: P(A AND B) = P(A) + P(B); Ha: P(A AND B) ≠ P(A) + P(B).
D. H0: The test is two-tailed; Ha: The test is right-tailed.
**Answer**: A

#### Right-Tailed Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test of independence is always ___ because large differences between observed and expected push the statistic into the right tail.
**Answer**: right-tailed

#### Right-Tailed Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why is the chi-square test of independence right-tailed?
A. Larger differences between observed and expected values produce larger chi-square statistics in the right tail.
B. Because the expected and observed values are always equal under H0.
C. Because we compare a left-tail probability to alpha.
D. Because df equals the sum of rows and columns.
**Answer**: A

#### Independence Of Events (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Two events A and B are independent if P(A AND B) = ___.
**Answer**: P(A)P(B)

#### Independence Of Events (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the 755-driver study, if speeding (A) and cell phone use (B) are independent, about how many drivers are expected to have both?
A. 28
B. 70
C. 305
D. 685
**Answer**: A

#### P-Value For Independence Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For this test, the p-value is ___ under the appropriate chi-square distribution.
**Answer**: P(χ² > observed test statistic)

#### P-Value For Independence Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In Example 11.6, with p-value = 0.0113 and α = 0.05, what is the decision?
A. Reject H0; the factors are not independent.
B. Fail to reject H0; the factors are independent.
C. Reject H0; the factors are independent.
D. Fail to reject H0; the test is left-tailed.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_147

#### Test For Homogeneity (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The ___ is used to decide whether two populations have the same distribution.
**Answer**: test for homogeneity

#### Test For Homogeneity (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which test is used to determine if two populations have the same distribution?
A. Test for homogeneity
B. Goodness-of-fit test
C. Test for independence
D. None of the above
**Answer**: A

#### Null Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a test for homogeneity, the null hypothesis states that the distributions of the two populations are ___.
**Answer**: the same

#### Null Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is the correct null hypothesis for comparing male and female living arrangements?
A. The distributions are the same.
B. The distributions are not the same.
C. Males prefer apartments.
D. The means are equal.
**Answer**: A

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis for a test for homogeneity is that the distributions of the two populations are ___.
**Answer**: not the same

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement represents Ha in a homogeneity test?
A. The two distributions are not the same.
B. The two distributions are the same.
C. Each expected cell value equals five.
D. The samples are independent.
**Answer**: A

#### Test Statistic (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test for homogeneity uses a ___ test statistic.
**Answer**: χ2

#### Test Statistic (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How is the homogeneity test statistic computed?
A. In the same way as the test for independence
B. By averaging the column totals
C. Using a t-statistic on means
D. By z-scores of proportions
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For the test of homogeneity, df = number of ___ − 1.
**Answer**: columns

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the living arrangements example with four response categories, what is df?
A. 3
B. 4
C. 2
D. 1
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Cell Frequency Condition (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: To use this test, the expected value for each cell must be at least ___.
**Answer**: five

#### Expected Cell Frequency Condition (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which condition must be met to validly use the homogeneity test?
A. Each expected cell value is at least five
B. The table total is exactly 100
C. The data are normally distributed
D. The sample sizes are equal
**Answer**: A

#### Common Uses (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A common use of the test for homogeneity is comparing ___.
**Answer**: two populations

#### Common Uses (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which scenario fits a common use of the homogeneity test?
A. Men vs. women on living arrangements
B. Checking if one die is fair
C. Estimating a population mean
D. Predicting exam scores
**Answer**: A

#### Applicable Variable Type (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The variable for a homogeneity test should be ___ with more than two possible response values.
**Answer**: categorical

#### Applicable Variable Type (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which variable meets the requirement for a homogeneity test?
A. Type of car driven
B. Height in inches
C. Temperature in degrees
D. Time to finish a race
**Answer**: A

#### Interpretation Limitation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: After rejecting H0 in a homogeneity test, we can say only that the distributions are ___; we cannot conclude how they differ.
**Answer**: not the same

#### Interpretation Limitation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the male vs. female living arrangements example at α = 0.05, what is the correct conclusion?
A. The distributions are not the same, but we cannot tell how they differ
B. Males and females have the same distribution
C. Males are more likely to live in dorms
D. Females are more likely to live with parents
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_149

#### Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis for the chi-square goodness-of-fit test states that the population ___ the given distribution.
**Answer**: fits

#### Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which chi-square test should you use to decide whether a single population’s outcomes fit a known distribution?
A. Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test
B. Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity
C. Chi-Square Test Of Independence
D. None of the above
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Test Of Independence (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the chi-square test of independence, H0 states that the two variables are ___.
**Answer**: independent

#### Chi-Square Test Of Independence (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: You have two qualitative variables and a contingency table; which chi-square test checks if the variables are related?
A. Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity
B. Chi-Square Goodness-Of-Fit Test
C. Chi-Square Test Of Independence
D. None of the above
**Answer**: C

#### Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test of homogeneity asks whether two populations follow the ___ distribution.
**Answer**: same

#### Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: A single qualitative question is asked of two different populations; which chi-square test compares their distributions?
A. Chi-Square Test Of Independence
B. Chi-Square Test Of Homogeneity

## Section: seg_151

#### Test Of A Single Variance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A test of a single variance assumes that the underlying distribution is ___.
**Answer**: normal

#### Test Of A Single Variance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which parameter is the focus of a test of a single variance?
A. the population variance (σ^2)
B. the sample variance (s^2)
C. the sample standard deviation (s)
D. the degrees of freedom (n - 1)
**Answer**: A

#### Null And Alternative Hypotheses For Single Variance Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If a student claims the exam standard deviation is more than five points, the alternative hypothesis is ___.
**Answer**: Ha: σ^2 > 5^2

#### Null And Alternative Hypotheses For Single Variance Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: An assistant thinks the standard deviation of dive depths is less than three feet. Which alternative hypothesis is correct?
A. Ha: σ^2 < 3^2
B. Ha: σ^2 > 3^2
C. Ha: σ^2 = 3^2
D. Ha: s^2 < 3^2
**Answer**: A

#### Test Statistic For Single Variance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test statistic for a single variance is ___.
**Answer**: (n - 1)s^2/σ^2

#### Test Statistic For Single Variance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using n = 25, s = 3.5, and σ = 7.2, what is the value of χ^2?
A. 5.67
B. 24.00
C. 11.34
D. 3.50
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The number of degrees of freedom for this test is ___.
**Answer**: n - 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For a sample of n = 25 customers, what are the degrees of freedom?
A. 24
B. 25
C. 23
D. 26
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Distribution For Single Variance Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sampling distribution of the test statistic is ___ with df = n - 1.
**Answer**: chi-square (χ^2)

#### Chi-Square Distribution For Single Variance Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement correctly describes the sampling distribution of the test statistic for a single-variance test?
A. It follows a chi-square (χ^2) distribution with df = n - 1.
B. It follows the distribution of s with df = n - 1.
C. It has no known distribution; use simulation.
D. It is always uniform on 0 to 1.
**Answer**: A

#### Random Variable In Single Variance Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In this test, the random variable is the sample ___ s.
**Answer**: standard deviation

#### Random Variable In Single Variance Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which quantity is treated as the random variable in a single variance test?
A. the sample standard deviation s
B. the population variance σ^2
C. the population standard deviation σ
D. the degrees of freedom (n - 1)
**Answer**: A

#### Tail Types For Single Variance Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In Example 11.11, the word "less" indicates a ___-tailed test.
**Answer**: left

#### Tail Types For Single Variance Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which alternative hypothesis leads to a right-tailed chi-square test?
A. Ha: σ^2 > stated value
B. Ha: σ^2 < stated value
C. Ha: σ^2 = stated value
D. H0: σ^2 > stated value
**Answer**: A

#### Sample Variance (s²)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: ___ : The variance computed from the sample.
**Answer**: Sample Variance (s²)

#### Population Variance (σ²)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: ___ : The variance of the population referenced in the hypotheses.
**Answer**: Population Variance (σ²)

B. To estimate the sample mean and standard deviation

D. To compute correlation between two variables
**Answer**: A

A. Chi-square
B. t
C. Normal
D. F
**Answer**: A

**Fill-in-the-blank**: For this lab, H0 states that the grocery receipts ___ the specified distribution.
**Answer**: follow

A. Receipts follow a uniform distribution over the observed range

C. Receipts follow a normal distribution with mean x̄
D. Receipts have increasing variance across intervals
**Answer**: A

**Fill-in-the-blank**: For this lab, Ha states that the grocery receipts do ___ the specified distribution.

B. Receipts do not follow the hypothesized distribution

B. Delete those cells from the analysis
C. Switch to a t-test
D. Double the sample values

## Section: seg_155

#### Chi-Square Test Of Independence (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test used to check if two categorical factors are related is called the ___.
**Answer**: chi-square test of independence

#### Chi-Square Test Of Independence (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation calls for a chi-square test of independence?
A. Checking if gender and favorite snack are related
B. Testing if data follow a normal distribution
C. Comparing two populations for the same distribution when it is unknown
D. Testing whether a population variance equals a target value
**Answer**: A

#### Null Hypothesis (Independence Test) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a chi-square test of independence, the null hypothesis states the two factors are ___.
**Answer**: independent

#### Null Hypothesis (Independence Test) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the correct null hypothesis when testing whether ticket class and travel distance are related?
A. Ticket class and travel distance are independent
B. Ticket class and travel distance are dependent
C. The two populations have the same distribution
D. The counts follow a specified distribution
**Answer**: A

#### Contingency Table (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A table that displays sample values for two factors that may be dependent is called a ___.
**Answer**: contingency table

#### Contingency Table (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does a contingency table help you determine?
A. Conditional probabilities
B. Population means
C. Linear correlation coefficients
D. Population variances
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Independence) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For a test of independence with i rows and j columns, df = ___.
**Answer**: (i − 1)(j − 1)

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Independence) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How many degrees of freedom does a 3 by 4 contingency table have?
A. 6
B. 12
C. 7
D. 24
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Cell Count (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The expected count in a cell is E = ___.
**Answer**: (row total)(column total) / total surveyed

#### Expected Cell Count (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a contingency table, the expected count for a cell equals:
A. (row total × column total) ÷ grand total
B. row total + column total
C. observed total ÷ number of cells
D. grand total ÷ 2
**Answer**: A

#### Expected Cell Frequency Condition (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Before using the test, each expected cell frequency should be at least ___.
**Answer**: five

#### Expected Cell Frequency Condition (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why might categories be combined in the snack-by-gender lab?
A. To ensure expected counts are at least five
B. To increase the sample mean
C. To reduce the population variance
D. To make the table square
**Answer**: A

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Independence) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square statistic for independence is χ² = ___.
**Answer**: Σ (O − E)² / E

#### Chi-Square Test Statistic (Independence) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the chi-square statistic, what do O and E represent?
A. O = observed counts, E = expected counts
B. O = expected counts, E = observed counts
C. O = observed counts, E = estimated residuals
D. O = outcomes per sample, E = overall mean
**Answer**: A

#### Right-Tailed Test (Independence) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The chi-square test of independence is a ___-tailed test.
**Answer**: right

#### Right-Tailed Test (Independence) (Q2)

A. Right tail only
B. Left tail only
C. Both tails
D. Center of the distribution
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_163

#### Line Of Best Fit (Least-Squares Regression Line) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The straight line that best fits the data by minimizing squared residuals is called the ___.
**Answer**: least-squares regression line

A. It minimizes the sum of squared residuals among all lines.
B. It passes through every data point.
C. Everyone will draw exactly the same by-eye line.
D. It maximizes the sum of squared residuals.
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: ŷ = a + bx

A. −173.51
B. 4.83
C. 173.51
D. −4.83
**Answer**: A

**Answer**: predicted value

A. ŷ is an estimate from the line and may not equal the observed y.
B. ŷ always equals the observed y.
C. ŷ is the same as the residual.
D. ŷ is the x-value rewritten.
**Answer**: A

B. The residual is positive, and the line overestimates y.

D. The residual is zero, and the line overestimates y.
**Answer**: A

D. 100% of the variation is explained because r > 0.6.
**Answer**: A

#### Best-Fit Line Passes Through (X̄, Ȳ) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The least-squares line always passes through the point ___.
**Answer**: (x̄, ȳ)

#### Best-Fit Line Passes Through (X̄, Ȳ) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is guaranteed for the least-squares regression line?
A. It passes through (x̄, ȳ).
B. It passes through the origin (0, 0).
C. It passes through every data point.
D. It has r = 1.
**Answer**: A

#### Independent And Dependent Variables (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the third exam vs final exam example, the independent variable is the ___ score.
**Answer**: third exam score

#### Independent And Dependent Variables (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the pinky length versus height activity, which is the dependent variable?
A. Height
B. Pinky length
C. Correlation r
D. SSE
**Answer**: A

#### Prediction Within Sample Domain (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: You should use the regression line to predict y only for x-values within the ___ of the sample data.
**Answer**: domain

#### Prediction Within Sample Domain (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In the exam data with x between 65 and 75, is predicting a final exam score for x = 50 appropriate?
A. No, because 50 is outside the sample x-domain.
B. Yes, because any x-value is acceptable.
C. Yes, because the line is straight.
D. Only if r = 1.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_165

#### Correlation Coefficient (r) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample correlation coefficient is denoted by ___.
**Answer**: r

#### Correlation Coefficient (r) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does r measure?
A. The strength and direction of the linear relationship between x and y
B. The slope of the regression line
C. The average of x values
D. The sample size
**Answer**: A

#### Population Correlation Coefficient (ρ) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The population correlation coefficient is denoted by ___.
**Answer**: ρ

#### Population Correlation Coefficient (ρ) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement about ρ is correct?
A. It is an unknown population parameter we test for being zero
B. It is always equal to r
C. It can be computed exactly from any sample
D. It measures only non-linear association
**Answer**: A

#### Significance Test For Correlation Coefficient (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test uses r and ___ together to decide if ρ differs from zero.
**Answer**: n

#### Significance Test For Correlation Coefficient (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the goal of the significance test for the correlation coefficient?
A. Decide whether ρ is significantly different from zero
B. Find the least-squares slope
C. Maximize r
D. Prove the line passes through all points
**Answer**: A

#### Null Hypothesis (H0: ρ = 0) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis for testing correlation states ___.
**Answer**: ρ = 0

#### Null Hypothesis (H0: ρ = 0) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In words, H0: ρ = 0 means:
A. There is no significant linear relationship in the population
B. r equals zero in the sample
C. The slope is positive
D. x causes y
**Answer**: A

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha: ρ ≠ 0) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis for testing correlation is ___.
**Answer**: ρ ≠ 0

#### Alternative Hypothesis (Ha: ρ ≠ 0) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In words, Ha: ρ ≠ 0 means:
A. A significant linear relationship exists in the population
B. The relationship is non-linear
C. r must equal 1 or -1
D. The regression line is unreliable
**Answer**: A

#### Significance Level (α) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In this chapter, we use a significance level of α = ___.
**Answer**: 0.05

#### Significance Level (α) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using the p-value method, we reject H0 when:
A. p-value < α
B. p-value > α
C. r = 0
D. df = n − 1
**Answer**: A

#### P-Value Method (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The p-value for testing correlation is calculated using a ___ with n - 2 degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: t-distribution

#### P-Value Method (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: At α = 0.05, which p-value leads to rejecting H0?
A. 0.026
B. 0.074
C. 0.50
D. 0.95
**Answer**: A

#### Critical Values Method (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the critical values method, r is significant when it lies ___ the interval between the negative and positive critical values.
**Answer**: outside

#### Critical Values Method (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For df = n - 2 at α = 0.05, which statement is correct?
A. If r > the positive critical value, r is significant
B. If r is between the negative and positive critical values, r is significant
C. Critical values do not depend on df
D. This method uses α = 0.10 in this chapter
**Answer**: A

#### Test Statistic (t) For Correlation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The test statistic t for correlation has the same sign as ___.
**Answer**: r

#### Test Statistic (t) For Correlation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula gives t for testing the significance of r?
A. t = r√(n - 2) / √(1 - r²)
B. t = r² / (n - 2)
C. t = (1 - r²) / √n
D. t = r√n / √(1 - r)
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: For testing the significance of r, the degrees of freedom are ___.
**Answer**: n - 2

#### Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If n = 11, what are the dfs for the test?
A. 9
B. 10
C. 11
D. 8
**Answer**: A

#### Significant Correlation Coefficient (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If r is significant, we conclude there is a ___ linear relationship between x and y.
**Answer**: significant

#### Significant Correlation Coefficient (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When r is significant and the scatter plot shows a linear trend, we can:
A. Use the regression line for prediction within the observed x-domain
B. Use the line to predict beyond any x values
C. Conclude y causes x
D. Ignore the significance level
**Answer**: A

#### Not Significant Correlation Coefficient (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If r is not significantly different from zero, we ___ use the regression line for modeling a linear relationship.
**Answer**: cannot

#### Not Significant Correlation Coefficient (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the test finds r not significant, the correct conclusion is:
A. Insufficient evidence of a significant linear relationship
B. There is a strong linear relationship
C. r must equal 1
D. The slope is zero in the sample
**Answer**: A

#### Conditions For Using The Regression Line For Prediction (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Do not use the regression line for prediction ___ the domain of observed x values.
**Answer**: outside

#### Conditions For Using The Regression Line For Prediction (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: We should use the regression line for prediction only when:
A. r is significant and the scatter plot shows a linear trend
B. r is not significant but the plot looks curved
C. There is any pattern at all
D. We want to extrapolate far beyond the data
**Answer**: A

#### Linearity In The Population (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assumption: The expected value of y for each x lies on a ___ in the population.
**Answer**: straight line

#### Linearity In The Population (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best states the linearity assumption?
A. The average y for each x lies on a straight line
B. All points lie exactly on a line
C. y values are uniformly distributed
D. x must be normally distributed
**Answer**: A

#### Normality Of Y About The Line (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assumption: For each x, y values are ___ distributed about the line.
**Answer**: normally

#### Normality Of Y About The Line (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does 'normality of y about the line' imply?
A. More y values lie near the line than far away
B. x values are normal
C. All y are equal
D. Residuals form a uniform distribution
**Answer**: A

#### Equal Standard Deviations (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assumption: The standard deviations of y about the line are ___ for all x.
**Answer**: equal

#### Equal Standard Deviations (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Equal standard deviations mean that:
A. Each normal distribution of y has the same spread about the line
B. All points have the same x
C. The slope equals zero
D. r equals 1
**Answer**: A

#### Independence Of Residuals (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assumption: The residual errors are mutually ___.
**Answer**: independent

#### Independence Of Residuals (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which pattern is consistent with independent residuals?
A. No pattern in the residuals
B. Strong cyclical pattern
C. Funnel-shaped spread
D. Perfect increasing trend
**Answer**: A

#### Random Sampling Or Randomized Experiment (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Assumption: The data come from a well-designed ___ or randomized experiment.
**Answer**: random sample

#### Random Sampling Or Randomized Experiment (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why is random sampling/assignment included as an assumption?
A. To ensure the inference from sample to population is appropriate
B. To force r to be large
C. To make df equal to n
D. Because it guarantees a causal relationship
**Answer**: A

A. ŷ = -173.51 + 4.83x

**Fill-in-the-blank**: In the third exam/final exam example, the observed x-values range from ___ to ___.

#### Domain Of Observed X Values (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which x-value is outside the domain of observed x-values for the third-exam data (65 to 75)?
A. 60
B. 65
C. 70
D. 75
**Answer**: A

#### Interpolation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Predicting inside of the observed x values in the data is called ___.

B. interpolation

**Fill-in-the-blank**: Predicting outside of the observed x values in the data is called ___.

## Section: seg_169

#### Outliers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Outliers are data points far from the least squares line and have large ___.
**Answer**: residuals

#### Outliers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best describes an outlier in a regression context?
A. A point far from the least squares line with a large residual.
B. A point far from other points in the x-direction that changes the slope.
C. A point very close to the best-fit line.
D. A point that always must be removed from the data.
**Answer**: A

#### Residual (Error) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A residual (error) is computed as ___.
**Answer**: y − ŷ

#### Residual (Error) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does a residual measure?
A. The vertical distance from a point to the best-fit line.
B. The horizontal distance between two x-values.
C. The slope of the regression line.
D. The correlation coefficient.
**Answer**: A

#### Influential Points (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Influential points are far from the other data mainly in the ___ direction.
**Answer**: horizontal

#### Influential Points (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How can you begin to identify an influential point?
A. Compare its residual to ±2s.
B. Test whether r is significant.
C. Remove it and see if the slope changes a lot.
D. Increase the sample size.
**Answer**: C

#### Standard Deviation Of Residuals (s) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When computing s from SSE, we divide by ___ because the regression uses two estimates.
**Answer**: n − 2

#### Standard Deviation Of Residuals (s) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How is s used to set an outlier threshold on a scatterplot?
A. Draw lines at ±s from the best-fit line.
B. Draw lines at ±2s parallel to the best-fit line.
C. Draw lines at ±3s through the origin.
D. Compare s directly to r.
**Answer**: B

#### Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SSE is the sum of the ___ residuals.
**Answer**: squared

#### Sum Of Squared Errors (SSE) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which quantity is directly used to compute s?
A. SSE.
B. The mean of x.
C. The sample size alone.
D. The correlation coefficient r.
**Answer**: A

#### Two-Standard-Deviation Rule For Outliers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A point is flagged when |y − ŷ| is greater than ___.
**Answer**: 2s

#### Two-Standard-Deviation Rule For Outliers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If s = 16.4, which residual indicates a potential outlier?
A. 20
B. 30
C. 35
D. −15
**Answer**: C

#### Graphical Identification Of Outliers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The graphical method draws two lines parallel to the best-fit line at ___ standard deviations above and below it.
**Answer**: two

#### Graphical Identification Of Outliers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes graphical identification of outliers?
A. Draw lines at ±2s parallel to the best-fit line and flag points outside.
B. Draw vertical lines at x ± 2s and flag points between them.
C. Compute SSE and compare it to r.
D. Look only for the largest x-value.
**Answer**: A

#### Numerical Identification Of Outliers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The numerical method compares each residual to ±___ to flag potential outliers.
**Answer**: 2s

#### Numerical Identification Of Outliers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which step is part of the numerical method for finding outliers?
A. Squaring residuals and dividing by n − 2 to get s directly for each point.
B. Compute each residual y − ŷ and compare its absolute value to 2s.
C. Zooming in on the graph to see if points cross Y2 and Y3.
D. Changing the correlation coefficient.
**Answer**: B

#### Handling Outliers (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: When you delete an outlier, you should provide results ___ the deleted data.
**Answer**: with and without

#### Handling Outliers (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is an appropriate action when an outlier is due to erroneous data?
A. Always keep it to preserve sample size.
B. Correct the value if possible or delete it, and document the change.
C. Change the slope so the point fits the line.
D. Ignore it because r is significant.
**Answer**: B

#### Residuals (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Residuals are the differences between observed y values and the corresponding ___ y values.

#### Residuals (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement best defines a residual?
A. The x-value of a point
B. The slope minus the intercept
C. The distance between actual y and estimated y
D. The percent of variation explained
**Answer**: C

#### Sum Of Squared Errors (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The ___ is the sum of squared residuals.
**Answer**: Sum of Squared Errors

#### Sum Of Squared Errors (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When the Sum of Squared Errors is minimized, what is determined?
A. The least-squares regression line
B. The y-intercept must be zero
C. r must equal 0
D. r² must equal 1
**Answer**: A

#### Coefficient Of Correlation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The correlation coefficient r must be between ___ and ___.
**Answer**: -1 and +1

#### Coefficient Of Correlation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If r is negative, what does that indicate?
A. x and y increase together
B. As x increases, y tends to decrease
C. There is no linear relationship
D. The data contain outliers
**Answer**: B

#### Coefficient Of Determination (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The coefficient of determination r² is equal to the ___ of the correlation coefficient.
**Answer**: square

#### Coefficient Of Determination (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does r² represent when expressed as a percent?
A. The percent change in x
B. The percent of variation in y explained by x via the regression line
C. The percent of points exactly on the line
D. The percent of outliers removed
**Answer**: B

#### Standard Deviation Of Residuals (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The standard deviation of the residuals, denoted ___, estimates the population standard deviation of y.
**Answer**: s

#### Standard Deviation Of Residuals (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The statistic s is computed from residuals using SSE and n − 2 and is used to estimate which quantity?
A. The slope b
B. The intercept a
C. The population standard deviation of y
D. The correlation r
**Answer**: C

#### Significance Testing Of Correlation Coefficient (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The most common null hypothesis for testing correlation is H0: ρ = ___.
**Answer**: 0

#### Significance Testing Of Correlation Coefficient (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which calculator function performs the linear regression t-test mentioned in the text?
A. LinRegTTest
B. Z-Test
C. Chi-Square GOF
D. ANOVA
**Answer**: A

#### Prediction Using Regression (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: After finding a strong correlation, you can use the least-squares line to make ___.
**Answer**: predictions

#### Prediction Using Regression (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When should you avoid using the regression line for prediction?
A. When x is within the data range
B. When making predictions for x-values outside the observed range
C. When r is close to ±1
D. When n is at least 20
**Answer**: B

#### Outlier (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An ___ is an observation that does not fit the rest of the data.
**Answer**: outlier

#### Outlier (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Using residuals, which rule flags an outlier?
A. If |residual| ≥ 2s
B. If residual = 0
C. If r = 0
D. If n < 30
**Answer**: A

#### Extrapolation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Making predictions for x-values outside the observed data range is called ___.
**Answer**: extrapolation

#### Extrapolation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Why doesn’t predicting the number of flu cases for 1970 using a model built on 1981–2002 data make sense?
A. There were no flu cases in 1970
B. The correlation was zero
C. 1970 is outside the data range, so it is extrapolation
D. The slope is undefined
**Answer**: C

## Section: seg_179

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A ___ test is used to determine if there is a statistically significant difference among several group means.
**Answer**: one-way ANOVA

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does a one-way ANOVA test investigate?
A. Whether there is a significant difference among several group means
B. The relationship between two numerical variables
C. Whether group medians are equal
D. Whether a single population is normal
**Answer**: A

#### Assumptions Of One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: One assumption for one-way ANOVA is that each ___ from which a sample is taken is normal.
**Answer**: population

#### Assumptions Of One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is NOT an assumption for a one-way ANOVA?
A. Samples are randomly selected and independent
B. Populations have equal standard deviations (or variances)
C. The factor is a categorical variable
D. The response is a categorical variable
**Answer**: D

#### Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, the null hypothesis states that all group population means are ___.
**Answer**: equal

#### Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is the null hypothesis in a one-way ANOVA?
A. All group population means are equal
B. At least two group means are not equal
C. The factor affects the response
D. The combined data variance is larger than each population variance
**Answer**: A

#### Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis in one-way ANOVA is that at least ___ of the group means are not equal.
**Answer**: two

#### Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is the alternative hypothesis in a one-way ANOVA?
A. μ1 = μ2 = ... = μk
B. At least two group population means are not equal
C. All populations are normal
D. Samples are independent
**Answer**: B

#### Factor (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, the factor is a ___ variable.
**Answer**: categorical

#### Factor (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the factor in a one-way ANOVA?
A. A numerical variable
B. A categorical variable
C. The population variance
D. The sample mean
**Answer**: B

#### Response (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, the response is a ___ variable.
**Answer**: numerical

#### Response (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is true about the response in a one-way ANOVA?
A. It is a numerical variable
B. It is a categorical variable
C. It must be the same across all groups
D. It must be normally distributed by itself
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_181

#### F Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F-tests in ANOVA use the ___ distribution with separate numerator and denominator degrees of freedom.
**Answer**: F distribution

#### F Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which notation correctly shows an F distribution with 4 numerator df and 10 denominator df?
A. F ~ F4,10
B. t ~ t4,10
C. F ~ F10,4
D. F ~ F14
**Answer**: A

#### F Statistic (F-Ratio) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F-ratio is computed as ___ divided by MSwithin.
**Answer**: MSbetween

#### F Statistic (F-Ratio) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Under the null hypothesis in one-way ANOVA, the F-ratio is expected to be approximately:
A. 0
B. 1
C. n − k
D. k − 1
**Answer**: B

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: One-Way ANOVA is preferred over multiple pairwise t-tests when comparing more than ___ groups.
**Answer**: two

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The one-way ANOVA hypothesis test is always:
A. Left-tailed
B. Two-tailed
C. Right-tailed
D. Depends on the data
**Answer**: C

#### Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, the null hypothesis states that all group population ___ are equal.
**Answer**: means

#### Null Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement matches H0 for one-way ANOVA?
A. All group population means are equal
B. At least two population means differ
C. All group variances are unequal
D. Samples are paired
**Answer**: A

#### Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The alternative hypothesis says that at least ___ of the sample groups come from populations with different normal distributions.
**Answer**: two

#### Alternative Hypothesis (One-Way ANOVA) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the alternative hypothesis for one-way ANOVA?
A. All group means are equal
B. At least two sample groups come from populations with different normal distributions
C. All groups are not normal
D. All variances are equal
**Answer**: B

#### Variance Between Samples (Between-Groups Variation) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Between-groups variation is also called variation due to ___ or explained variation.
**Answer**: treatment

#### Variance Between Samples (Between-Groups Variation) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When group sizes are equal, the variance between samples equals the variance of the sample means multiplied by:
A. n
B. k
C. n − k
D. dfbetween
**Answer**: A

#### Variance Within Samples (Within-Groups Variation) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Within-groups variation is also called variation due to ___ or unexplained variation.
**Answer**: error

#### Variance Within Samples (Within-Groups Variation) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: The variance within samples is estimated by the ___ of the sample variances.
A. sum
B. average
C. median
D. maximum
**Answer**: B

#### Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SSbetween is the sum of squares that represents the variation ___ the different samples.
**Answer**: among

#### Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which expression equals SSbetween (using group sums s_j and sizes n_j, total n)?
A. Σ[(s_j)^2 / n_j] − (Σ s_j)^2 / n
B. Σ x^2 − (Σ x)^2 / n
C. SStotal − SSbetween
D. SSwithin / dfwithin
**Answer**: A

#### Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SSwithin is computed as SStotal minus ___.
**Answer**: SSbetween

#### Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: SSwithin represents variation within samples due to:
A. treatment
B. chance
C. measurement units
D. between-group differences
**Answer**: B

#### Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SStotal = ∑x^2 − (∑x)^2 / ___.
**Answer**: n

#### Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which description matches SStotal?
A. Sum of squares of all values minus the square of the total sum divided by n
B. Sum of squares representing variation among groups
C. Sum of sample variances divided by dfwithin
D. SStotal − SSbetween
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Square (MS) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: MSbetween equals SSbetween divided by ___.
**Answer**: dfbetween

#### Mean Square (MS) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: MSwithin is computed as:
A. SSwithin / dfwithin
B. SSbetween / dfbetween
C. SSwithin / dfbetween
D. SSbetween / dfwithin
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom Between (dfbetween) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, dfbetween = ___ − 1.
**Answer**: k

#### Degrees Of Freedom Between (dfbetween) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If there are k groups, dfbetween equals:
A. k − 1
B. n − k
C. n − 1
D. k
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom Within (dfwithin) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, dfwithin = ___ − k.
**Answer**: n

#### Degrees Of Freedom Within (dfwithin) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If total sample size is n and there are k groups, dfwithin is:
A. n − k
B. k − 1
C. n − 1
D. n + k
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_183

#### F Distribution Shape (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F distribution is not symmetrical but ___ to the right.
**Answer**: skewed

#### F Distribution Shape (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which best describes the shape of the F distribution?
A. Symmetric and bell-shaped
B. Skewed to the right and not symmetrical
C. Uniform over an interval
D. Skewed to the left
**Answer**: B

#### F Distribution Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F distribution has a different curve for each set of ___.
**Answer**: numerator and denominator degrees of freedom

#### F Distribution Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: How does the F distribution change with degrees of freedom?
A. It is the same for all degrees of freedom
B. It has a different curve for each set of numerator and denominator degrees of freedom
C. It depends only on the numerator mean
D. It depends only on total sample size
**Answer**: B

#### F Distribution Notation (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F distribution is denoted as ___, with subscripts for numerator and denominator dfs.
**Answer**: Fdf(num),df(denom)

#### F Distribution Notation (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which notation correctly specifies an F distribution with numerator and denominator degrees of freedom?
A. Fdf(num),df(denom)
B. t(df)
C. N(μ, σ^2)
D. χ2(df)
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom In One-Way ANOVA F-Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, df(num) = ___ and df(denom) = ___.
**Answer**: k - 1; n - k

#### Degrees Of Freedom In One-Way ANOVA F-Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: For k groups and total sample size n in one-way ANOVA, the degrees of freedom are:
A. Numerator k - 1; Denominator n - k
B. Numerator n - k; Denominator k - 1
C. Numerator n - 1; Denominator k - 1
D. Numerator k; Denominator n - k
**Answer**: A

#### F Statistic (F Ratio) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, the F statistic equals ___ / ___.
**Answer**: MSbetween / MSwithin

#### F Statistic (F Ratio) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In one-way ANOVA, the F statistic is defined as:
A. MSbetween divided by MSwithin
B. SSbetween divided by SStotal
C. MSwithin divided by MSbetween
D. Mean of group means divided by pooled variance
**Answer**: A

#### P-Value For F Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The p-value for an F test is computed as ___.
**Answer**: P(F > observed F)

#### P-Value For F Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Given an observed F statistic F0, the p-value for the ANOVA F test is:
A. P(F > F0)
B. 2P(|F| > F0)
C. P(F < F0)
D. P(|F| ≥ F0)
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_185

#### F Test Of Two Variances (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A procedure using the ___ distribution to compare two population variances.
**Answer**: F distribution

#### F Test Of Two Variances (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which situation calls for a test of two variances?
A. Comparing average grades between two instructors
B. Comparing variability in grades between two instructors
C. Testing whether a single class has mean grade 75
D. Estimating the overall mean check-out time
**Answer**: B

#### Assumptions For Two-Variance F Test (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The two-variance F test assumes the populations are ___.
**Answer**: normally distributed and independent

#### Assumptions For Two-Variance F Test (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is required to use the F test for two variances?
A. Two independent normal populations
B. Large samples from any distributions
C. Paired (dependent) samples from normal populations
D. Equal sample sizes only
**Answer**: A

#### F Statistic For Two Variances (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Under H0: σ1^2 = σ2^2, the F statistic simplifies to ___.
**Answer**: s1^2 / s2^2

#### F Statistic For Two Variances (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which formula matches the F statistic in general?
A. (s1^2/σ1^2) / (s2^2/σ2^2)
B. s1 / s2
C. (s1^2 + s2^2) / 2
D. σ1^2 / σ2^2
**Answer**: A

#### F Distribution And Degrees Of Freedom (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: If sample sizes are n1 and n2, then F ~ F(___, ___).
**Answer**: n1 − 1, n2 − 1

#### F Distribution And Degrees Of Freedom (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If n1 = n2 = 30, which F distribution applies?
A. F30,30
B. F29,29
C. F28,28
D. F1,58
**Answer**: B

#### Hypotheses And Tail Direction (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Testing H0: σ1^2 = σ2^2 versus Ha: σ1^2 < σ2^2 is a ___-tailed test.
**Answer**: left

#### Hypotheses And Tail Direction (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which alternative hypothesis leads to a two-tailed test?
A. Ha: σ1^2 < σ2^2
B. Ha: σ1^2 > σ2^2
C. Ha: σ1^2 ≠ σ2^2
D. Ha: σ1^2 = σ2^2
**Answer**: C

#### Orientation Of F Ratio (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: Depending on Ha and which sample variance is larger, the F ratio may be s1^2/s2^2 or ___.
**Answer**: s2^2/s1^2

#### Orientation Of F Ratio (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If you put the larger sample variance in the numerator, F will be:
A. close to zero
B. close to one
C. greater than one
D. negative
**Answer**: C

#### Interpretation Of F Magnitude (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: An F value close to ___ favors the null hypothesis of equal variances.
**Answer**: one

#### Interpretation Of F Magnitude (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement is consistent with interpreting F?
A. F much larger than 1 (with the larger sample variance on top) suggests unequal variances.
B. F near 0 proves the variances are equal.
C. F negative indicates strong evidence against H0.
D. F exactly 1 always rejects H0.
**Answer**: A

#### Sensitivity To Non-Normality (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F test for two variances is very sensitive to ___ from normality.
**Answer**: deviations

#### Sensitivity To Non-Normality (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: If the two distributions are not normal, what can happen?
A. p-values may be unpredictably higher or lower than they should be.
B. The test automatically becomes a t test.
C. There is no effect as long as n1 = n2.
D. The test statistic cannot be computed.
**Answer**: A

## Section: seg_187

#### Analysis Of Variance (ANOVA) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In ANOVA, the test statistic is the ___.
**Answer**: F-ratio

#### Analysis Of Variance (ANOVA) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does ANOVA test?
A. Whether the means of three or more populations are equal
B. Whether two population variances are equal
C. Whether three or more population proportions are equal
D. Whether three or more population medians are equal
**Answer**: A

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: A one-way ANOVA has ___.
**Answer**: one independent variable and one dependent variable

#### One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: When is a one-way ANOVA appropriate?
A. One independent variable, one dependent variable, normal populations with equal standard deviations, and random, independent samples
B. Two independent variables and one dependent variable
C. One categorical and one quantitative variable with paired samples
D. Any number of variables as long as sample sizes are equal
**Answer**: A

#### Variance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The sample variance equals the sum of squared deviations divided by ___.
**Answer**: n − 1

#### Variance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which statement defines variance?
A. The mean of the squared deviations from the mean
B. The square root of the mean
C. The average absolute deviation from the median
D. The ratio of two standard deviations
**Answer**: A

#### F Distribution (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F distribution is always ___ and skewed right.
**Answer**: positive

#### F Distribution (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is a property of the F distribution?
A. Always positive and skewed right
B. Symmetric about zero
C. Can take negative values
D. Uniform on a finite interval
**Answer**: A

#### F Statistic (F-Ratio) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The F statistic compares variation among group means to variation ___ the groups.
**Answer**: within

#### F Statistic (F-Ratio) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In one-way ANOVA, the F statistic is computed as:
A. MSbetween divided by MSwithin
B. MSwithin divided by MSbetween
C. SStotal divided by SSwithin
D. SSbetween divided by SStotal
**Answer**: A

#### Assumptions For One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: One assumption for one-way ANOVA is that the populations have equal ___.
**Answer**: standard deviations

#### Assumptions For One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which is NOT an assumption of one-way ANOVA?
A. Each population is normal
B. Samples are randomly selected and independent
C. Populations have equal standard deviations
D. All group means are equal
**Answer**: D

#### Hypotheses In One-Way ANOVA (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The null hypothesis in one-way ANOVA states that all group ___ are equal.
**Answer**: means

#### Hypotheses In One-Way ANOVA (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What is the alternative hypothesis in one-way ANOVA?
A. At least one group mean is different from the others
B. All group variances are equal
C. All group means are equal
D. All samples are paired
**Answer**: A

#### Degrees Of Freedom (F Distribution) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In one-way ANOVA, df(num) = ___.
**Answer**: k − 1

#### Degrees Of Freedom (F Distribution) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: With three groups and 20 total observations, what is df(denom) for the F test?
A. 17
B. 18
C. 16
D. 19
**Answer**: A

#### Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SSbetween measures variation ___ group means.
**Answer**: among

#### Sum Of Squares Between (SSbetween) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: SSbetween is used to compute which quantity in ANOVA?
A. MSbetween
B. MSwithin
C. SStotal
D. Pooled variance
**Answer**: A

#### Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SSwithin = SStotal − ___.
**Answer**: SSbetween

#### Sum Of Squares Within (SSwithin) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does SSwithin measure?
A. Variation within groups
B. Variation among group means
C. Variation between samples and populations
D. Variation only due to treatments
**Answer**: A

#### Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: SStotal measures the total variation across all ___.
**Answer**: observations

#### Total Sum Of Squares (SStotal) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which relationship holds in one-way ANOVA?
A. SStotal = SSbetween + SSwithin
B. SStotal = SSbetween − SSwithin
C. SStotal = MSbetween + MSwithin
D. SStotal = MSbetween − MSwithin
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Square Between (MSbetween) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: MSbetween = SSbetween divided by ___.
**Answer**: dfbetween

#### Mean Square Between (MSbetween) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a one-way ANOVA with k groups, MSbetween equals SSbetween divided by:
A. k − 1
B. n − k
C. n − 1
D. k
**Answer**: A

#### Mean Square Within (MSwithin) (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: MSwithin = SSwithin divided by ___.
**Answer**: dfwithin

#### Mean Square Within (MSwithin) (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: In a one-way ANOVA with n total observations and k groups, MSwithin equals SSwithin divided by:
A. n − k
B. k − 1
C. n − 1
D. k
**Answer**: A

#### Test Of Two Variances (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: In a test of two variances, the F statistic uses numerator and denominator degrees of freedom that are one less than the corresponding ___ sizes.
**Answer**: sample

#### Test Of Two Variances (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: Which assumption is required for the F test of two variances?
A. Both populations are normally distributed
B. Both population means are equal
C. Samples are paired
D. Large samples are required
**Answer**: A

#### Pooled Variance (Q1)
**Fill-in-the-blank**: The pooled variance is the mean of the ___.
**Answer**: sample variances

#### Pooled Variance (Q2)
**Multiple Choice**: What does s^2 pooled represent in the formula review?
A. The mean of the sample variances
B. The variance of the pooled mean
C. The average of the group means
D. The within-group mean square
**Answer**: A
